Remark 2.21
.

Although
T
-operads are defined for any operad
T
on a Cartesian category
\scr{E}
, Leinster’s contractions are defined only on globular
T
-collections, where
T
is an arbitrary monad on the Cartesian category
\scr{Q}
of globular
\omega
-sets.
As a consequence, proposition
2.20
holds actually for any Cartesian operad
T
on the Cartesian category of globular
\omega
-sets
\scr{Q}
. We will use this fact, substituting the free strict globular
\omega
-category monad
\hat{T}
with the free involutive strict globular
\omega
-category monad
\hat{T}^{\star}
in the next section
3.2
.

\lrcorner

Remark 2.22
.

As anticipated in footnote
8
, following the treatment in
[
Leinster 2004
, definition 9.2.1]
, we did not include in our definition of globular contracted
\hat{T}
-operad, in the last point of proposition
2.20
, any further compatibility axioms between contractions and operadic units/multiplications.

We introduce here a more restrictive notion of
globular
\hat{T}
-operadic contraction
over
\bullet
, consisting of data
(P,\pi_{P},\kappa_{P},\eta_{P},\mu_{P})
such that
(P,\pi_{P},\kappa_{P})
is a globular contracted
\hat{T}
-collection in
\scr{Q}^{\hat{T},\kappa}_{\bullet}
and
(P,\pi_{P},\eta_{P},\mu_{P})
is a globular
\hat{T}
-operad in
\scr{O}^{\hat{T}}_{\bullet}
that furthermore satisfy the commutativity of the following diagrams in
\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}}_{\bullet}
:

\textstyle{\Par(\pi_{\bullet})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{\bullet}}
\scriptstyle{\Par_{\eta_{P}}}
\textstyle{\Par(\pi_{P})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{P}}
\textstyle{\bullet\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{P}}
\textstyle{P}
\textstyle{\Par(\pi_{P}\circ^{2}_{0}\pi_{P})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{P}\circ^{2}_{0}\kappa_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\Par_{\mu_{P}}}
\textstyle{\Par(\pi_{P})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{P}}
\textstyle{P\circ^{1}_{0}P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{P}}
\textstyle{P}

where
\bullet\xrightarrow{\pi_{\bullet}}\hat{T}(\bullet)
is
\pi_{\bullet}:=\eta^{T}_{\bullet}
, we have
P\circ^{1}_{0}P\xrightarrow{\pi_{P}\circ^{2}_{0}\pi_{P}}\hat{T}(\bullet)\circ^{1}_{0}\hat{T}(\bullet)
and lemma
2.19
provides
\Par_{\eta_{P}}
and
\Par_{\mu_{P}}
.
9
9

9

Notice that the contraction
\kappa_{\bullet}:(x^{+},y,x^{-})\mapsto y
is uniquely determined by the defining axioms of contraction and the definition of
\pi_{\bullet}
.

The category
\scr{O}\scr{K}^{\hat{T}}_{\bullet}
of globular
\hat{T}
-operadic contractions over
\bullet
is just the full subcategory of
\scr{O}^{\hat{T},\kappa}_{\bullet}
determined by the objects defined above.

It is perfectly viable to use the category
\scr{O}\scr{K}^{\hat{T}}_{\bullet}
instead of
\scr{O}^{\hat{T},\kappa}_{\bullet}
in order to define a slightly more restrictive notion of weak globular
\omega
-category as an algebra for the initial object in
\scr{O}\scr{K}^{\hat{T}}_{\bullet}
, whose existence can be obtained following similar steps and in the case examined in the subsequent subsection
2.3.3
.

\lrcorner

2.3.3
Weak
\omega
-categories

The subsequent fundamental result is proved in
[
Leinster 2004
, appendix G]
.

Theorem 2.23
.

The category
\scr{O}^{\hat{T},\kappa}_{\bullet}
of globular contracted
\hat{T}
-operads has initial objects.

The proof provided in the reference above is not direct, and is based on the following definitions and results.

Definition 2.24
.

A strict 1-category
{\mathscr{C}}
is
filtered
if any finite diagram
{\mathscr{D}}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{D}}{\mathscr{C}}
admits a co-cone.
10
10

10

This is equivalent to requiring:
a) that there exists at least an object in
{\mathscr{C}}^{0}
(the vertex of a co-cone on the empty diagram);
b) for any two objects
A_{1},A_{2}\in{\mathscr{C}}^{0}
, there exists a co-cone
A_{1}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{1}}C\xleftarrow{\alpha_{2}}A_{2}
;
c) for any finite diagram consisting of pair of parallel arrows
A_{1}\xrightarrow{f,g}A_{2}
, there exists a co-cone
A_{1}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{1}}C\xleftarrow{\alpha_{2}}A_{1}
and hence
\alpha_{2}\circ f=\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{2}\circ g
.

A
filtered co-limit
is a co-limit for a diagram
{\mathscr{D}}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{D}}{\mathscr{B}}
with filtered 1-category
{\mathscr{D}}
.

A
finitary functor
is a functor
{\mathscr{A}}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{F}}{\mathscr{B}}
that preserves filtered co-limits.

A
finitely presentable object
is an object
A\in{\mathscr{C}}^{0}
whose covariant
\Hom
-functor
\Hom_{\mathscr{C}}(A,-)
is finitary.

A category
{\mathscr{C}}
is
locally finite presentable
if the following properties are satisfied:

\blacktriangleright

{\mathscr{C}}
is co-complete (it admits all co-limits of diagrams
{\mathscr{D}}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{D}}{\mathscr{C}}
with small
{\mathscr{D}}
),

\blacktriangleright

the full subcategory of finitely presentable objects of
{\mathscr{C}}
is essentially small,
11
11

11

Recall that in a
locally small category

{\mathscr{C}}
, for all objects
A,B\in{\mathscr{C}}^{0}
,
\Hom_{\mathscr{C}}(A,B)
is a set; a locally small category is
small
if and only if
{\mathscr{C}}^{0}
is a set; a category is
essentially small
if and only if it equivalent to a small category.

\blacktriangleright

every object of
{\mathscr{C}}
is a filtered co-limit of at least one diagram of finitely presentable objects.

This first lemma is a consequence of
[
Leinster 2004
, theorems 6.5.1, 6.5.2 and 6.5.4 in appendix D]
.

Lemma 2.25
.

If
T
is a finitary Cartesian monad on a Cartesian category
\scr{E}
, the forgetful functor
\scr{O}^{T}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{U}}\scr{E}^{T}_{\bullet}
has a left adjoint and the adjunction is monadic.

In particular, from proposition
2.11
, we have that
\scr{O}^{\hat{T}}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{U}}\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}}_{\bullet}
is monadic.

Furthermore
\mathfrak{U}
is finitary
[
Leinster 2004
, appendix G page 353]
.

This second lemma follows from a direct construction contained in
[
Leinster 2004
, appendix G page 352]
.

Lemma 2.26
.

The forgetful functor
\scr{Q}^{\hat{T},\kappa}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{W}}\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}}_{\bullet}
is finitary, has a left adjoint and the adjunction is monadic.

This third lemma is dealt with in
[
Leinster 2004
, appendix G page 352]
using that
\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}}_{\bullet}
is a pre-sheaf category.

Lemma 2.27
.

The category
\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}}_{\bullet}
is locally finitely presentable.

Lemma 2.28
.

[
Kelly 1980
, proposition 27.1]

In the (big) category
\scr{C}
of functors between 1-categories, consider the co-span
{\mathscr{A}}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathscr{X}}\xleftarrow{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathscr{B}}
and assume that:

\blacktriangleright

{\mathscr{X}}
is a locally finitely presentable 1-category,

\blacktriangleright

the functors
\mathfrak{A}
and
\mathfrak{B}
are both finitary and monadic,

\blacktriangleright

the span
{\mathscr{A}}\xleftarrow{\hat{\mathfrak{A}}}{\mathscr{W}}\xrightarrow{\hat{\mathfrak{B}}}{\mathscr{B}}
is a strict pull-back of of the diagram
{\mathscr{A}}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathscr{X}}\xleftarrow{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathscr{B}}

\textstyle{{\mathscr{W}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hat{\mathfrak{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\hat{\mathfrak{B}}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{B}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{B}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{A}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{A}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{X}}.}

.

The functor
{\mathscr{W}}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{A}\circ\hat{\mathfrak{A}}=\mathfrak{B}\circ\hat{\mathfrak{B}}}{\mathscr{X}}
is also monadic.

As a consequence
[
Leinster 2004
, corollary G.1.2]
the category
{\mathscr{W}}
has an initial object.

Theorem
2.23
follows applying the previous lemmata in the case of the strict pull-back of forgetful functors:

\textstyle{\scr{O}^{\hat{T},\kappa}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hat{\mathfrak{U}}_{\kappa}}
\scriptstyle{\hat{\mathfrak{U}}{O}}
\textstyle{\scr{O}^{\hat{T}}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{U}{O}}
\textstyle{\scr{Q}^{\hat{T},\kappa}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{U}_{\kappa}}
\textstyle{\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}}_{\bullet}.}

Here is the basic notion of weak
\omega
-category from
[
Leinster 2004
, definition III.9.2.3]
.

Definition 2.29
.

A
weak
\omega
-category
is an algebra for any monad
L
, that is an initial object in
\scr{O}^{\hat{T},\kappa}_{\bullet}
.

2.3.4
Examples

The basic way to provide examples of weak globular
\omega
-categories in Leinster’s approach is described in
[
Leinster 2004
, Example 9.2.4]
: any contracted
\hat{T}
-operad
(P,\kappa,\mu,\eta)\in\scr{O}^{\hat{T},\kappa}_{\bullet}
(this means for every choice of a contraction on a monad over
\bullet
in the bicategory
\scr{Q}_{\hat{T}}
) induces (since
L
is initial) a unique morphism
L\xrightarrow{!}P
in
\scr{O}^{\hat{T},\kappa}
that produces a strict-functor from algebras over
P
to algebras over
L
(that are, by definition, weak globular
\omega
-categories).
Hence an example of weak globular
\omega
-category is obtained as soon as we are given:

\blacktriangleright

(P,\pi,\kappa,\mu,\eta)
a contracted
\hat{T}
-operad (that is just a contraction on a monad in
\scr{Q}_{\hat{T}}
),

\blacktriangleright

an algebra
X\in\scr{Q}_{\hat{T}}
for the contracted
\hat{T}
-operad
P
(that is just an algebra for the previous monad).

Notable examples of weak globular
\omega
-categories in Leinster’s approach include:

\blacktriangleright

strict globular
\omega
-categories (see example
[
Leinster 2004
, 9.2.5]
): these are just algebras for the terminal contracted
\hat{T}
-operad
\hat{T}(\bullet)\in\scr{O}^{\hat{T},\kappa}
,
12
12

12

The terminal object in
\scr{O}^{\hat{T},\kappa}_{\bullet}
is
\bullet\xleftarrow{!}\hat{T}(\bullet)\xrightarrow{\iota(\hat{T}(\bullet))}\hat{T}(\bullet)
, we will describe in more detail this point for our new monad
\hat{T}^{\star}
in remark
3.11
.

\blacktriangleright

the globular
\omega
-homotopy groupoid
\Pi_{\omega}(S)
of a topological space
S
: in
[
Leinster 2004
, example 9.2.7]
it is shown that, for every topological space
S
, the
\omega
-homotopy groupoid
\Pi_{\omega}(S)
is an algebra for a certain contracted-
\hat{T}
-operad.

3
Involutive Leinster Weak Globular
\omega
-categories

In the present section we proceed to study how to introduce weak involutions on a weak globular
\omega
-category.

We start in subsection
3.1
from involutions on strict globular
\omega
-categories, as defined in our previous paper
[
Bejrakarbum Bertozzini 2017
]
that extended to the
\omega
-category case the notion of fully involutive strict globular
n
-category introduced in
[
Bertozzini Conti Lewkeeratiyutkul Suthichitranont 2020
, section 4]
.

Subsection
3.2
contains our tentative definition of involutive weak globular
\omega
-category based on an initial generalized operad
L^{\star}
in the bicategory
\scr{Q}_{\hat{T}^{\star}}
induced by the free involutive globular omega category monad
\hat{T}^{\star}
over the same Cartesian bicategory
\scr{Q}
of globular
\omega
-sets.

3.1
Involutive Strict Globular
\omega
-categories

We quickly recall the basic definitions and contructions of free strict involutive globular
\omega
-categories, closely following our previous paper
[
Bejrakarbum Bertozzini 2017
]
.

Definition 3.1
.

A (reflexive)
\omega
-quiver
Q:=Q^{0}\overset{s^{0}}{\underset{t^{0}}{\leftleftarrows}}Q^{1}\overset{s^{1}}{\underset{t^{1}}{\leftleftarrows}}\cdots\overset{s^{n-2}}{\underset{t^{n-2}}{\leftleftarrows}}Q^{n-1}\overset{s^{n-1}}{\underset{t^{n-1}}{\leftleftarrows}}Q^{n}\overset{s^{n}}{\underset{t^{n}}{\leftleftarrows}}\cdots
is
fully self-dual
if it is equipped with a family
(\gamma_{q})_{q\in{\mathbb{N}}}
endomorphisms
Q\xrightarrow{\gamma_{q}}Q
, for all
q\in{\mathbb{N}}
, such that:

\blacktriangleright

for all
q\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\gamma_{q}
is
q
-contravariant:
\gamma^{q}_{q}\circ s^{q}=t^{q}\circ\gamma_{q}^{q+1}
,
\gamma^{q}_{q}\circ t^{q}=s^{q}\circ\gamma_{q}^{q+1}
,

\blacktriangleright

for all
p,q\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that
p\neq q
,
\gamma_{q}
is
p
-covariant:

\gamma^{p}_{q}\circ s^{p}=s^{p}\circ\gamma_{q}^{p+1}
,
\gamma^{p}_{q}\circ t^{p}=t^{p}\circ\gamma_{q}^{p+1}
.

A (reflexive) globular
\omega
-set (respectively a (reflexive) globular
\omega
-magma) is said to be self-dual whenever its underlying
\omega
-quiver is self-dual in the previous sense.
13
13

13

Here we interpret
\circ,\iota,\gamma
as partially defined binary, nullary, unary operations, and we do not impose any algebraic axiom for magmas.

A strict globular
\omega
-category
({\mathscr{C}},\circ,\iota)
that is also a fully self-dual
\omega
-quiver
({\mathscr{C}},*)
is a
fully involutive strict globular
\omega
-category
if its family
(*_{q})_{q\in{\mathbb{N}}}
of self-duality endomorphisms (here denoted by
x\mapsto x^{*^{k}_{q}}
, for
x\in{\mathscr{C}}^{k}
and
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
) satisfies the following algebraic axioms:

\blacktriangleright

(
q
-contravariance
):
(x\circ^{k}_{q}y)^{*^{k}_{q}}=y^{*^{k}_{q}}\circ^{k}_{q}x^{*^{k}_{q}}
,
\forall k,q\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that
0\leq q<k
and
(x,y)\in{\mathscr{C}}^{k}\times_{{\mathscr{C}}^{q}}{\mathscr{C}}^{k}
,

(
q
-contravariance
):

(x\circ^{k}_{q}y)^{*^{k}_{p}}=x^{*^{k}_{p}}\circ^{k}_{q}y^{*^{k}_{p}}
,
\forall k,p,q\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that
0\leq q\neq p<k
and
(x,y)\in{\mathscr{C}}^{k}\times_{{\mathscr{C}}^{q}}{\mathscr{C}}^{k}
,

\blacktriangleright

(
unitality
):
(\iota^{k}(x))^{*^{k+1}_{q}}=\iota^{k}(x^{*^{k}_{q}})
,  for all
k,q\in{\mathbb{N}}
and for all
x\in{\mathscr{C}}^{k}
,

\blacktriangleright

(
involutivity
):
(x^{*^{k}_{q}})^{*^{k}_{q}}=x
,  for all
k,q\in{\mathbb{N}}
, and all
x\in{\mathscr{C}}^{k}
,

\blacktriangleright

(
commutativity
):
(x^{*^{k}_{q}})^{*^{k}_{p}}=(x^{*^{k}_{p}})^{*^{k}_{q}}
,  for all
k,p,q\in{\mathbb{N}}
, and all
x\in{\mathscr{C}}^{k}
,

\blacktriangleright

(
q
-grounding
):
x^{*^{k}_{q}}=x
,  for all
k,q\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that
k\leq q
and all
x\in{\mathscr{C}}^{k}
.

A
self-dual morphism
between self-dual
\omega
-quivers (and similarly between self-dual (reflexive) globular
\omega
-sets or self-dual (reflexive) globular
\omega
-magmas) is a morphism
(Q,\gamma)\xrightarrow{\phi}(\hat{Q},\hat{\gamma})
such that
\hat{\gamma}\circ\phi=\phi\circ\gamma
.

An
involutive
\omega
-functor

{\mathscr{C}}\xrightarrow{\phi}\hat{{\mathscr{C}}}
between fully involutive strict globular
\omega
-categories is just a self-dual morphism for the underlying self-dual globular
\omega
-magmas.

This involutive version of proposition
2.3
was proved in
[
Bejrakarbum Bertozzini 2017
, propositions 3.1, 3.2]
.

Proposition 3.2
.

Let
\scr{C}^{\star}
denote the strict 1-category of covariant involutive
\omega
-functors between strict globular fully involutive
\omega
-categories.

For any globular
\omega
-set
Q
in
\scr{Q}
, a
free strict involutive globular
\omega
-category over
Q
is a morphism of globular
\omega
-sets
Q\xrightarrow{\eta^{\star}_{Q}}\mathfrak{U}^{\star}({\mathscr{C}})
, into the underlying globular
\omega
-set
\mathfrak{U}^{\star}({\mathscr{C}})
of a strict involutive globular
\omega
-category
{\mathscr{C}}
, satisfying the following universal factorization property:
for any morphism of globular
\omega
-sets
Q\xrightarrow{\phi}\mathfrak{U}^{\star}(\hat{{\mathscr{C}}})
, into the underlying globular
\omega
-set
\mathfrak{U}^{\star}(\hat{{\mathscr{C}}})
of a strict involutive globular
\omega
-category
\hat{{\mathscr{C}}}
, there exists a unique involutive
\omega
-functor
{\mathscr{C}}\xrightarrow{\hat{\phi}}\hat{{\mathscr{C}}}
of fully involutive strict globular
\omega
-categories such that
\phi=\hat{\phi}\circ\eta^{\star}_{Q}
.

The
forgeful functor

\scr{C}^{\star}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{U}^{\star}}\scr{Q}
(forgetting compositions, identities and involutions of objects in
\scr{C}^{\star}
) admits a left-adjoint
\mathfrak{F}^{\star}\dashv\mathfrak{U}^{\star}

free strict involutive globular
\omega
-category functor

{\mathscr{C}}^{\star}\xleftarrow{\mathfrak{F}^{\star}}\scr{Q}
that is uniquely determined via a specific construction of free involutive strict globular
\omega
-category
Q\xrightarrow{\eta^{\star}_{Q}}\mathfrak{U}^{\star}({\mathscr{C}})
over a globular
\omega
-set
Q
.

Proof.

For convenience of the reader, we recall an explicit construction of the free strict globular involutive
\omega
-category
Q\xrightarrow{\eta^{\star}_{Q}}\mathfrak{F}^{\star}(Q)
over a given globular
\omega
-set
Q
.

a.

We first produce the free globular self-dual relfexive
\omega
-magma
Q\xrightarrow{\zeta_{Q}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)
, over the globular
\omega
-set
Q
, with respect to all the partial binary compositions, the unary involutions and the nullary identities involved in the definition of strict globular involutive
\omega
-category.

b.

Then we consider in
\mathfrak{M}(Q)
the smallest congruence relation of globular self-dual reflexive
\omega
-magmas
\Xi\subset\mathfrak{M}(Q)\times\mathfrak{M}(Q)
containing all the pairs of terms appearing into the algebraic axioms that are involved in the definition of strict globular involutive
\omega
-category.

c.

Finally, considering the quotient morphism
\mathfrak{M}(Q)\xrightarrow{\varpi}\mathfrak{M}(Q)/\Xi
of globular self-dual reflexive
\omega
-magmas and the map of globular
\omega
-sets
Q\xrightarrow{\varpi\circ\zeta_{Q}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)/\Xi
, we notice that
\mathfrak{F}^{\star}(Q):=\mathfrak{M}(Q)/\Xi
is a strict involutive globular
\omega
-category
and that
\eta^{\star}_{Q}:=\varpi\circ\zeta_{Q}
satisfies the universal factorization property for free involutive globular
\omega
-categories over
Q
.

a.

The free globular self-dual reflexive
\omega
-magma
Q\xrightarrow{\zeta_{Q}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)
over the globular
\omega
-set
Q
is obtained by a recursive definition.
We first provide a 1-quiver
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{0}\leftleftarrows\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}
.

Starting from
Q^{0}
, we construct
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{0}:=Q^{0}
.
We then introduce
Q^{0}_{\iota}:=\{(x,\iota^{0})\ |\ x\in Q^{0}\}
(that is a disjoint copy of
Q^{0}
representing freely added identities of elements in
Q^{0}
) and
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[1]^{0}:=Q^{1}\uplus Q^{0}_{\iota}
with source/target given by
s^{0}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x):=s^{0}_{Q}(x)
,
t^{0}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x):=t^{0}_{Q}(x)
, for all
x\in Q^{1}
and
s^{0}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x,\iota):=x=:t^{0}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x,\iota)
, for all
x\in Q^{0}
.
If
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[1]^{j}
has been already constructed, we define
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[1]^{j+1}:=\{(x,\gamma_{0})\ |\ x\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[1]^{j}\}
(that introduces freely added self-dualities of elements in
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[1]^{j}
) with source
s^{0}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x,\gamma_{0}):=t^{0}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x)
and target
t^{0}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x,\gamma_{0}):=s^{0}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x)
; defining
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[1]:=\bigcup_{j=1}^{+\infty}\mathfrak{M}^{1}(Q)[1]^{j}
, we get a 1-quiver
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{0}\leftleftarrows\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[1]
.

We proceed to define
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[2]^{0}:=\Big\{(x,0,y)\ |\ (x,y)\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[1]\times_{\mathfrak{M}^{0}(Q)}\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[1]\Big\}
(whose elements represent freely added compositions) with sources and targets

s_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}^{0}(x,0,y):=s_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}^{0}(y)
and
t_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}^{0}(x,0,y):=t_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}^{0}(x)
.
Exactly as done before, we define
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[2]:=\bigcup_{j=1}^{+\infty}\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[2]^{j}
, that freely introduces arbitrary iterations of
\gamma_{0}
-self-dualities of the 1-cells in
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[2]^{0}
, obtaining a new 1-quiver
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{0}\leftleftarrows\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[1]\cup\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[2]
.

Supposing, by recursion, that the 1-quiver
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{0}\leftleftarrows\bigcup_{n=1}^{k}\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[n]
is already defined, we extend it freely adding compositions
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[k+1]^{0}:=\{(x,0,y)\ |\ (x,y)\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[i]\times_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{0}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[j],\ i+j=k+1\}
, with sources/targets
s_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}^{0}(x,0,y):=s_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}^{0}(y)
,
t_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}^{0}(x,0,y):=t_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}^{0}(x)
, and freely adding arbitrary iterations of
\gamma_{0}
-self-dualities to get
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[k+1]:=\bigcup_{j=1}^{+\infty}\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[k]^{j}
and the 1-quiver
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{0}\leftleftarrows\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}
, where we have
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}:=\bigcup_{k=1}^{+\infty}\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[k]
.

The previous 1-quiver, becomes reflexive defining
\iota^{0}:\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{0}\to\mathfrak{M}(Q^{1})
by
x\mapsto(x,\iota^{0})
; it further becomes self-dual defining
\gamma_{0}:\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}\to\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}
by
x\mapsto(x,\gamma_{0})
; a binary composition
\circ^{1}_{0}:\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}\times_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{0}}\mathfrak{M}(Q^{1})\to\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}
is also present defining
\circ^{1}_{0}:(x,y)\mapsto(x,0,y)
and hence we have a reflexive self-dual (globular) 1-magma.

Supposing by recursion that a reflexive self-dual globular magma for the
n
-quiver
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{0}\leftleftarrows\cdots\leftleftarrows\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}
has been already defined, we will obtain a reflexive self-dual globular magma
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{0}\leftleftarrows\cdots\leftleftarrows\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\leftleftarrows\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}
.

We start with
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}[0]^{0}:=Q^{n+1}\cup\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}_{\iota}
, where
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}_{\iota}:=\{(x,\iota^{n})\ |\ x\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\}
with sources and targets
s_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x,\iota^{n}):=x=:t_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x,\iota^{n})
; we then introduce
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}[0]^{j+1}:=\{(x,\gamma_{q})\ |\ x\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}[0]^{j},\ q=0,\dots,n\}
, with sources/targets
s_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}/t_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x,\gamma_{q})=s_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}/t_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x)
, if
q<n
, and, for
q=n
,
s_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x,\gamma_{n})=t_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x)
,
t_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x,\gamma_{n})=s_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x)
; and we get
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}[0]:=\bigcup_{j=1}^{+\infty}\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}[0]^{j}
.
Next, assuming for recursion that
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}[k]
(with its source and target maps) have been already defined, we introduce free depth-
p
compositions
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}[k+1]^{0}:=\Big\{(x,p,y)\ |\ p=0,\dots,n,\ (x,y)\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}[i]\times_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{p}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}[j],\ i+j=k+1\Big\}
, with source targets

s_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x,p,y):=(s_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x),p,s_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(y))
and
t_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x,p,y):=(t_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x),p,t_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(y))
, whenever
0\leq p<n
, and otherwise
s_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x,n,y):=s_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(y)
and
t_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x,p,y):=t_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x)
.
Subsequently we introduce free iterated self-duals of the previous
(n+1)
-cells by
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}[k+1]:=\bigcup_{j=0}^{+\infty}\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}[k+1]^{j}
where, as above, we consider
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}[k=1]^{j+1}:=\Big\{(x,\gamma_{q})\ |\ q=0,\dots,n,\ x\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}[k+1]^{j}\Big\}
with similarly defined source/target maps.
Finally we set
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}:=\bigcup_{k=0}^{+\infty}\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}[k]
obtaining the required globular
(n+1)
-quiver that is reflexive, with the map
\iota^{n}(x):=(x,\iota^{n})
, for
x\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}
; self-dual with the maps
x\mapsto(x,\gamma_{q})
, for all
x\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}
and all
q\in\{0,\dots,n\}
; and a magma for the partial compositions
x\circ^{n+1}_{p}y:=(x,p,y)
, for all
(x,y)\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}\times_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{p}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}
and all
p\in\{0,\dots,n\}
.

The recursive construction of the globular reflexive self-dual
\omega
-magma
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{0}\leftleftarrows\cdots\leftleftarrows\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\leftleftarrows\cdots
is now completed and we further produce the morphism
Q\xrightarrow{\eta_{Q}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)
of globular
\omega
-sets by the inclusion of
Q^{n}
into
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}[0]^{0}\subset\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}
, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
.

We only need to check the universal factorization property for the morphism
Q\xrightarrow{\eta_{Q}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)
.
Suppose that
Q\xrightarrow{\phi}\hat{M}
is a morphism of reflexive self-dual globular
\omega
-sets into a reflexive self-dual globular
\omega
-magma. Any grade-preserving map
\mathfrak{M}(Q)\xrightarrow{\hat{\phi}}\hat{M}
such that
\phi=\hat{\phi}\circ\eta_{Q}
must necessarily satisfy
x\mapsto\phi(x)
, for all
x\in Q^{n}\subset\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}
. Using the fact the
\hat{\phi}
must be a morphism of reflexive self-dual globular
\omega
-magmas, by induction, we obtain that, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\hat{\phi}(x,\iota^{n})=\hat{\iota}_{\hat{M}}^{n}(\phi(x))
, for all
x\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}
,
\hat{\phi}(x,p,y)=\phi(x)\hat{\circ}^{n}_{p}\phi(y)
, for all
(x,y)\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\times_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{p}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}
and
0\leq p<n
, and
\hat{\phi}(x,\gamma_{q})=\phi(x)^{*^{n}_{q}}
, for all
x\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}
and
0\leq q<n
.
This uniquely defined map
\mathfrak{M}(Q)\xrightarrow{\hat{\phi}}\hat{M}
is a morphism of relfexive self-dual globular
\omega
-magmas as required.

b.

From
[
Bejrakarbum Bertozzini 2017
, section 3.2]
we recall that, given globular
\omega
-sets
(Q,s_{Q},t_{Q})
,
(\hat{Q},s_{\hat{Q}},t_{\hat{Q}})
the
Cartesian product of globular
\omega
-sets
is the globular
\omega
-set
(Q\times\hat{Q},s_{Q\times\hat{Q}},t_{Q\times\hat{Q}})
defined, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, as
(Q\times\hat{Q})^{n}:=Q^{n}\times\hat{Q}^{n}
, with source/targets
s^{n}_{Q\times\hat{Q}}:=(s^{n}_{Q},s^{n}_{\hat{Q}})
and
t^{n}_{Q\times\hat{Q}}:=(t^{n}_{Q},t^{n}_{\hat{Q}})
acting componentwise; and that whenever
(Q,s_{Q},t_{Q},\iota_{Q},\gamma_{Q},\circ_{Q})
,
(\hat{Q},s_{\hat{Q}},t_{\hat{Q}},\iota_{\hat{Q}},\gamma_{\hat{Q}},\circ_{\hat{Q}})
are globular self-dual reflexive
\omega
-magmas, also their Cartesian product
Q\times\hat{Q}
is a globular self-dual reflexive
\omega
-magma with the componentwise defined nullary
\iota^{n}_{Q\times\hat{Q}}:=(\iota^{n}_{Q},\iota^{n}_{\hat{Q}})
, unary
\gamma^{(Q\times\hat{Q})^{n}}_{q}:=(\gamma^{Q^{n}}_{q},\gamma_{q}^{\hat{Q}^{n}})
and binary

\circ_{p}^{(Q\times\hat{Q})^{n}}:=(\circ_{p}^{Q^{n}},\circ_{p}^{\hat{Q}^{n}})
operations.

We also recall that a
congruence

X
in a globular self-dual reflexive
\omega
-magma
M
is a globular self-dual reflexive
\omega
-magma
X
such that
X^{n}\subset M^{n}\times M^{n}
, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, in such a way that the inclusion
X\xrightarrow{\phi}M\times M
is a morphism of globular self-dual reflexive
\omega
-magmas.

Inside the free globular self-dual reflexive
\omega
-magma
\mathfrak{M}(Q)
over
Q
constructed in a. above we consider the congruence
\Xi\subset\mathfrak{M}(Q)\times\mathfrak{M}(Q)
generated
14
14

14

Since intersection of congruences is a congruence,
\Xi
is just the intersection of all the conguences containing the given pairs.

by the union of all of the following families of pairs:

\displaystyle\Big\{(x\circ^{n}_{p}(y\circ^{n}_{p}z),\ (x\circ^{n}_{p}y)\circ^{n}_{p}z)\quad\big|\quad n>p\in{\mathbb{N}},\ (x,y,z)\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\times_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{p}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\times_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{p}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\Big\}

\displaystyle\Big\{((\iota^{n-1}\circ\cdots\iota^{p}\circ t^{p}\circ\cdots\circ t^{n-1}(x))\circ^{n}_{p}x,\ x)\quad\big|\quad n>p\in{\mathbb{N}},\ x\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\Big\}

\displaystyle\Big\{(x,\ x\circ^{n}_{p}(\iota^{n-1}\circ\cdots\iota^{p}\circ s^{p}\circ\cdots\circ s^{n-1}(x)))\quad\big|\quad n>p\in{\mathbb{N}},\ x\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\Big\}

\displaystyle\Big\{(\iota^{n}(x)\circ^{n+1}_{p}\iota^{n}(y),\ \iota^{n}(x\circ^{n}_{p}y))\quad\big|\quad n>p\in{\mathbb{N}},\ (x,y)\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\times_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{q}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\Big\}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Big\{((x\circ^{n}_{p}y)\circ^{n}_{q}(z\circ^{n}_{p}w),\ \ &(x\circ^{n}_{q}z)\circ^{n}_{p}(y\circ^{n}_{q}w))\quad\big|\quad n>p,q\in{\mathbb{N}},\ \\
&(x,y),(z,w)\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\times_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{p}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n},\ (x,z),(y,w)\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\times_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{q}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\Big\}\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\Big\{(\gamma^{n}_{q}(\gamma^{n}_{q}(x)),\ x)\quad\big|\quad n>q\in{\mathbb{N}},\ x\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\Big\}\quad\quad\Big\{(\gamma_{q}^{n}(\gamma_{p}^{n}(x)),\ \gamma_{p}^{n}(\gamma_{q}^{n}(x)))\quad\big|\quad n>q,p\in{\mathbb{N}},\ x\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\Big\}

\displaystyle\Big\{(\gamma^{n}_{q}(x\circ^{n}_{p}y),\ \gamma^{n}_{q}(x)\circ^{n}_{p}\gamma^{n}_{q}(y))\quad\big|\quad n>p\neq q\in{\mathbb{N}},\ (x,y)\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\times_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{p}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\Big\}

\displaystyle\Big\{(\gamma^{n}_{q}(x\circ^{n}_{p}y),\ \gamma^{n}_{q}(y)\circ^{n}_{p}\gamma^{n}_{q}(x))\quad\big|\quad n>p=q\in{\mathbb{N}},\ (x,y)\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\times_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{p}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\Big\}

\displaystyle\Big\{(\iota^{n}(\gamma^{n}_{q}(x)),\ \gamma^{n+1}_{q}(\iota^{n}_{q}(x)))\quad\big|\quad n>q\in{\mathbb{N}},\ x\in\mathfrak{M}^{n}(Q)\Big\}\quad\quad\Big\{(\gamma^{n}_{q}(x),\ x)\quad\big|\quad n\leq q\in{\mathbb{N}},\ x\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\Big\}.

c.

As every
quotient of a globular self-dual relfexive
\omega
-magma by a globular
\omega
-congruence
, the quotient
\mathfrak{M}(Q)/\Xi
is a globular
\omega
-set with
(\mathfrak{M}(Q)/\Xi)^{n}:=\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}/\Xi^{n}=\Big\{[x]_{\Xi^{n}}\ |\ x\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\Big\}
and
s^{n}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)/\Xi}([x]_{\Xi^{n+1}}):=[s^{n}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x)]_{\Xi^{n}}
,

t^{n}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)/\Xi}([x]_{\Xi^{n+1}}):=[t^{n}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x)]_{\Xi^{n}}
, for
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
x\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}
; and
\mathfrak{M}(Q)/\Xi
is actually a globular self-dual reflexive
\omega
-magma with well-defined compositions given by
[x]_{\Xi^{n}}\circ^{(\mathfrak{M}(Q)/\Xi)^{n}}_{p}[y]_{\Xi^{n}}:=[x\circ^{\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}}_{p}y]_{\Xi^{n}}
,
self-dualties
\gamma_{q}([x]_{\Xi^{n}}):=[\gamma_{q}(x)]_{\Xi^{n}}
, and reflexive maps

\iota^{n}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)/\Xi}([x]_{\Xi^{n}}):=[\iota^{n}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x)]_{\Xi^{n+1}}
,
for
n>p\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
q\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
x,y\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}
.
Funrthermore, since all the algebraic axioms for strict globular involutive
\omega
-category are already included in
\Xi
, we have that the quotient
\mathfrak{M}(Q)/\Xi
is actually a strict globular involutive
\omega
-category and the quotient morphism
\varpi:\mathfrak{M}(Q)\to\mathfrak{M}(Q)/\Xi
, defined as
\varpi^{n}(x):=[x]_{\Xi^{n}}
, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
x\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}
, is a morphism of globular self-dual reflexive
\omega
-sets.

We only need to check that the morphism of globular
\omega
-sets
Q\xrightarrow{\eta^{\star}_{Q}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)/\Xi
, with
\eta^{\star}_{Q}:=\varpi\circ\zeta_{Q}
, into the globular involutive
\omega
-category
\mathfrak{M}(Q)/\Xi
, satisfies the universal factorization property.
For any other morphism
Q\xrightarrow{\phi}{\mathscr{C}}
of globular self-dual reflexive
\omega
-sets, into a strict globular involutive
\omega
-category
{\mathscr{C}}
, since
Q\xrightarrow{\zeta_{Q}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)
is a free globular self-dual reflexive
\omega
-magma, there exists a unique morphism
\mathfrak{M}(Q)\xrightarrow{\overline{\phi}}{\mathscr{C}}
of globular self-dual reflexive
\omega
-magmas such that
\phi=\overline{\phi}\circ\zeta_{Q}
.
Consider now the
kernel globular
\omega
-congruence

\Xi_{\overline{\phi}}
induced by the morphism
\overline{\phi}
of
\omega
-magmas: for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, we have
\Xi_{\overline{\phi}}^{n}:=\Big\{(x,y)\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\times\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\ |\ \overline{\phi}(x)=\overline{\phi}(y)\Big\}
and, since all the axioms of strict globular involutive
\omega
-category are already satisfied in
{\mathscr{C}}
, we have
\Xi\subset\Xi_{\overline{\phi}}
and hence
\mathfrak{M}(Q)/\Xi_{\overline{\phi}}
is already a strict globular involutive
\omega
-category, furthermore the assignment
\tilde{\phi}:[x]_{\Xi_{\overline{\phi}}}\mapsto\overline{\phi}(x)
is a well-defined covariant involutive
\omega
-functor
\mathfrak{M}(Q)/\Xi_{\overline{\phi}}\xrightarrow{\tilde{\phi}}{\mathscr{C}}
, that is actually the unique such that
\overline{\phi}=\tilde{\phi}\circ\varpi_{\phi}
, where
\mathfrak{M}(Q)\xrightarrow{\varpi_{\phi}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)/\Xi_{\overline{\phi}}
is the quotient morphism of globular self-dual reflexive
\omega
-magmas defined as usual by
\varpi_{\phi}:x\mapsto[x]_{\Xi_{\overline{\phi}}}
, for
x\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)
. From
\Xi\subset\Xi_{\overline{\phi}}
, we obtain a unique involutive
\omega
-functor
\mathfrak{M}(Q)/\Xi_{\overline{\phi}}\xrightarrow{\theta}\mathfrak{M}(Q)/\Xi
,
\theta:[x]_{\Xi}\mapsto[x]_{\Xi_{\overline{\phi}}}
, for
x\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)
, such that
\varpi_{\phi}\circ\varpi=\theta
. Defining
\hat{\phi}:=\tilde{\phi}\circ\theta
, we have that
\mathfrak{M}(Q)/\Xi\xrightarrow{\hat{\phi}}{\mathscr{C}}
is the unique involutive
\omega
-functor such that
\hat{\phi}\circ\eta^{\star}_{Q}=\tilde{\phi}\circ\theta\circ\varpi\circ\zeta_{Q}=\tilde{\phi}\circ\varpi_{\phi}\circ\eta_{Q}=\overline{\phi}\circ\eta_{Q}=\phi
.
∎

Remark 3.3
.

The existence of algebras (with a given signature) that are free over a set is known;
15
15

15

See for example section 6 in the n-Lab entry:
https://ncatlab.org/nlab/show/variety+of+algebras#literature
.

propositions
2.3
and
3.2
are essentially special cases of a general existence theorem for “
\omega
-algebras” that are free over a globular
\omega
-set that (for the
\omega
-globular setting) is vertically categorifying the case of algebras over sets.

\lrcorner

In paralled with corollary
2.9
, from proposition
3.2
we obtain a new monad in the involutive
\omega
-category case.

Corollary 3.4
.

On the 1-category
\scr{Q}
of morphisms of globular
\omega
-sets, we have the following

\blacktriangleright

free involutive strict globular
\omega
-category monad

\hat{T}^{\star}:=\mathfrak{U}^{\star}\circ\mathfrak{F}^{\star}
.

3.2
Involutive Weak Globular
\omega
-categories

The following is the “involutive case” version of proposition
2.11
.

Proposition 3.5
.

The 1-category
\scr{Q}
of small globular
\omega
-sets with morphisms of globular
\omega
-sets
is Cartesian.
The 1-category
\scr{C}^{\star}
of small strict globular involutive
\omega
-categories with involutive
\omega
-functors is Cartesian.
The forgetful 1-functor
\scr{C}^{\star}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{U}^{\star}}\scr{Q}
and the free strict globular involutive
\omega
-category 1-functor
\scr{C}^{\star}\xleftarrow{\mathfrak{F}^{\star}}\scr{Q}
are Cartesian.
The free strict globular involutive
\omega
-category monad
T^{\star}:=\mathfrak{U}^{\star}\circ\mathfrak{F}^{\star}
is Cartesian.

Proof.

The Cartesianity of the 1-category
\scr{Q}
of globular
\omega
-sets is already known by proposition
2.11
. Following the exposition in
[
Bejrakarbum 2023
, section 3.2]
, we recall that, given a span
Q\xrightarrow{\phi}X\xleftarrow{\psi}R
of globular
\omega
-sets in
\scr{Q}
, a pull-back can be constructed via the co-span of globular
\omega
-sets
Q\xleftarrow{\hat{\psi}}Q\times_{X}R\xrightarrow{\hat{\phi}}R
defined by

Q\times_{X}R:=\left((Q\times_{X}R)^{n}\overset{s^{n}}{\underset{t^{n}}{\leftleftarrows}}(Q\times_{X}R)^{n+1}\right)_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
where

(Q\times_{X}R)^{n}:=Q^{n}\times_{X^{n}}R^{n}:=\{(q,r)\in Q^{n}\times R^{n}\ |\ \phi^{n}(q)=\psi^{n}(r)\}
,

s^{n}(q,r):=(s^{n}_{Q}(q),s^{n}_{R}(r))
and
t^{n}(q,r):=(t^{n}_{Q}(q),t^{n}_{R}(r))
, with
\hat{\phi}^{n}(q,r):=r
and
\hat{\psi}^{n}(q,r):=q
, for
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
.

To prove the Cartesianity of the 1-category
\scr{C}^{\star}
, from
[
Bejrakarbum 2023
, section 3.3]
, we recall that given any co-span
{\mathscr{A}}\xrightarrow{\phi}{\mathscr{X}}\xleftarrow{\psi}{\mathscr{B}}
in
\scr{C}^{\star}
, a pull-back can be constructed via the previous span
{\mathscr{A}}\xleftarrow{\hat{\psi}}{\mathscr{A}}\times_{\mathscr{X}}{\mathscr{B}}\xrightarrow{\hat{\phi}}{\mathscr{B}}
in
\scr{Q}
, noting that the globular
\omega
-set
{\mathscr{A}}\times_{\mathscr{X}}{\mathscr{B}}
becomes a strict involutive globular
\omega
-category, with componentwise
compositions
(a_{1},b_{1})\circ^{n}_{q}(a_{2},b_{2}):=(a_{1}\ {\circ_{\mathscr{A}}}^{n}_{q}\ a_{2},b_{1}\ {\circ_{\mathscr{A}}}^{n}_{q}\ b_{2})
,
identities
\iota^{n}(a,b):=(\iota^{n}_{\mathscr{A}}(a),\iota_{\mathscr{B}}^{n}(b))
, involutions
(a,b)^{*_{q}}:=(a^{*_{q}^{\mathscr{A}}},b^{*_{q}^{\mathscr{B}}})
; and that the above-defined
\hat{\phi}
and
\hat{\psi}
turn out to be involutive covariant
\omega
-functors.

From the previous explicit definitions of pull-backs in
\scr{Q}
and
\scr{C}^{\star}
, it follows that the forgetful functor
\mathfrak{U}^{\star}
is Cartesian, since it associates to the standard pull-back of strict involutive globular
\omega
-categories the standard pull-back of their underlying globular
\omega
-sets.

In order to prove the Cartesianity of the free strict involutive globular
\omega
-category functor
\mathfrak{F}^{\star}
, we simply notice that

\mathfrak{F}^{\star}(Q)\xleftarrow{\mathfrak{F}^{\star}(\hat{\psi})}\mathfrak{F}^{\star}(Q\times_{X}R)\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{F}^{\star}(\hat{\phi})}\mathfrak{F}^{\star}(R)
is canonically
\scr{C}^{\star}
-isomorphic to the standard
\scr{C}^{\star}
-pull-back
\mathfrak{F}^{\star}(Q)\xleftarrow{\widehat{\mathfrak{F}^{\star}(\psi)}}\mathfrak{F}^{\star}(Q)\times_{\mathfrak{F}^{\star}(X)}\mathfrak{F}^{\star}(R)\xrightarrow{\widehat{\mathfrak{F}^{\star}(\phi)}}\mathfrak{F}^{\star}(R)
of the co-span
\mathfrak{F}^{\star}(Q)\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{F}^{\star}(\phi)}\mathfrak{F}^{\star}(X)\xleftarrow{\mathfrak{F}^{\star}(\psi)}\mathfrak{F}^{\star}(R)
, via an involutive
\omega
-functor.

The composition of Cartesian functors is Cartesian, hence the Cartesianity of the monad
T^{\star}:=\mathfrak{U}^{\star}\circ\mathfrak{F}^{\star}
.

For the Cartesianity of the natural transformation
\eta^{\star}
, we must show that, for any morphism
Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\phi}Q_{2}
of globular
\omega
-sets, the solid square commuting diagram below is a pull-back in
\scr{Q}
; for this purpose, for any span
Q_{2}\xleftarrow{\beta}R\xrightarrow{\alpha}\hat{T}^{\star}(Q_{1})
such that
\hat{T}^{\star}(\phi)\circ\alpha=\eta^{\star}_{Q_{2}}\circ\beta
, we must see that there exists a unique morphism
R\xrightarrow{\theta}Q_{1}
making commutative the two triangle diagrams below:
\alpha=\eta^{\star}_{Q_{1}}\circ\theta
,
\beta=\phi\circ\theta
.

\textstyle{R\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\scriptstyle{\theta}
\textstyle{Q_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{\star}_{Q_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\hat{T}^{\star}(Q_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hat{T}^{\star}(\phi)}
\textstyle{Q_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{\star}_{Q_{2}}}
\textstyle{\hat{T}^{\star}(Q_{2})}

From the explicit construction of the free involutive globular
\omega
-category of a globular
\omega
-set recalled in proposition
3.2
, for all
x\in Q
, we have that
\eta^{\star}_{Q}(x)=\varpi\circ\zeta_{Q}(x)=[x]_{\Xi}
is a singleton containing only
x
.
Hence, for all
r\in R
,
\eta^{\star}_{Q_{2}}(\beta(r))=[x_{2}]_{\Xi_{2}}
is a singleton in
\hat{T}^{\star}(Q_{2})
, with
x_{2}\in Q_{2}
; since
\hat{T}^{\star}(\phi)
, as every morphism of globular
\omega
-sets, is “degree-preserving”, there exists a unique element
\theta(r)\in Q_{1}^{0}
such that
(\hat{T}^{\star}(\phi))([\theta(r)]_{\Xi_{1}})=[x_{2}]_{\Xi_{2}}
. Such
\theta
satisfies our requirements.

For the Cartesianity of the natural transformation
\mu_{Q}
,
we must show that, for any morphism
Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\phi}Q_{2}
of globular
\omega
-sets, the solid square commuting diagram below is a pull-back in
\scr{Q}
; for this purpose, for any span
(\hat{T}^{\star}\circ\hat{T}^{\star})(Q_{2})\xleftarrow{\beta}R\xrightarrow{\alpha}Q_{1}
such that
\phi\circ\alpha=\mu^{\star}_{Q_{2}}\circ\beta
, we must see that there exists a unique morphism
R\xrightarrow{\theta}(\hat{T}^{\star}\circ\hat{T}^{\star})(Q_{1})
making commutative the two triangle diagrams below:
\alpha=\mu^{\star}_{Q_{1}}\circ\theta
and
\beta=((\hat{T}^{\star}\circ\hat{T}^{\star})(\phi))\circ\theta
.

\textstyle{R\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\scriptstyle{\theta}
\textstyle{(\hat{T}^{\star}\circ\hat{T}^{\star})(Q_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{\star}_{Q_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{(\hat{T}^{\star}\circ\hat{T}^{\star})(\phi)}
\textstyle{\hat{T}^{\star}(Q_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hat{T}^{\star}(\phi)}
\textstyle{(\hat{T}^{\star}\circ\hat{T}^{\star})(Q_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{\star}_{Q_{2}}}
\textstyle{\hat{T}^{\star}(Q_{2})}

Since for all
r\in R
, we have
\alpha(r)\in\hat{T}^{\star}(Q_{1})
, the only possible element in
(\hat{T}^{\star}\circ\hat{T}^{\star})(Q_{1})
that maps, via
\mu^{\star}_{Q_{1}}
to
\alpha(r)
, must necessarily be
(\alpha(r))
and the assignment
r\mapsto(\alpha(r))
is a morphism of globular
\omega
-sets satisfying the required conditions.
∎

As direct application of proposition
2.12
to the Cartesian monad
\hat{T}^{\star}
on the Cartesian category
\scr{Q}
we obtain:

Corollary 3.6
.

There is a bicategory
\scr{Q}_{\hat{T}^{\star}}
.

The notion of Leinster contraction in definition
2.17
remains unchanged and, as anticipated in remark
2.21
, we have a parallel version of definition
2.18
and proposition
2.20
that reformulate as follows, for the case of the free involutive globular
\omega
-category monad
\hat{T}^{\star}
.

Definition 3.7
.

Let
\bullet\in\scr{Q}
denote a terminal object in the category of globular
\omega
-sets.

A
globular
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection
is a morphism
Q\xrightarrow{\pi}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
in
\scr{Q}
;
a
globular contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection

consists of a Leinster contraction
\kappa
on a globular
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection
\pi
:

\Par(\pi)\xrightarrow{\kappa}Q\xrightarrow{\pi}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
.

Similarly, using the bicategory
\scr{Q}_{\hat{T}^{\star}}
, for the Cartesian monad
\hat{T}^{\star}
, definition
2.16
already provides the notion of globular (contracted)
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operad over
\bullet
.
For technical reasons, in the proof of the subsequent theorem
3.16
, we actually need to introduce the following more general “magma structure” internal to
\scr{Q}_{\hat{T}^{\star}}
.

Definition 3.8
.

A
globular
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magma

16
16

16

Of course, although the definition is here given in the special case of the bicategory
\scr{Q}_{\hat{T}^{\star}}
, it remains perfectly valid when applied to an arbitrary bicategory
\scr{E}_{T}
, for a given Cartesian monad
T
; furthermore one can define
multicategorical magmas over
E
using a given object
E
in place of a terminal
\bullet
in
\scr{E}_{T}
.

(M,\pi_{M},\eta_{M},\mu_{M})

over
\bullet
is a 1-cell
\bullet\xrightarrow{M}\bullet
in the bicategory
\scr{Q}_{\hat{T}^{\star}}
(hence a globular
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection
M\xrightarrow{\pi_{M}}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
) that is equipped with a unit 2-cell
\bullet\xrightarrow{\eta_{M}}M
and a multiplication 2-cell
M\circ^{1}_{0}M\xrightarrow{\mu_{M}}M

as specified in the following commutative diagrams

in the category

that are not necessarily required to satisfy the operadic axioms
2.3
.

A
globular
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operad
is a globular
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magma with unit and multiplication that satisfy the monadic associativity and unitality axioms
2.3
.

Proposition 3.9
.

For any terminal object
\bullet\in\scr{Q}^{0}
, we have the following categories:

\blacktriangleright

the
category
\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}
of globular
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collections over
\bullet
,

\blacktriangleright

the
category
\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}^{\star},\kappa}_{\bullet}
of globular contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collections over
\bullet
,

\blacktriangleright

the
category
\scr{M}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}
of globular
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magmas
over
\bullet
,

\blacktriangleright

the
category
\scr{O}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}
of globular
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operads over
\bullet
,

\blacktriangleright

the
category
\scr{M}^{\hat{T}^{\star},\kappa}_{\bullet}
of globular contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magmas over
\bullet
,

\blacktriangleright

the
category
\scr{O}^{\hat{T}^{\star},\kappa}_{\bullet}
of globular contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operads over
\bullet
.

There are commuting diagram (in the category of functors between 1-categories) of forgetful functors:

\textstyle{\scr{O}^{\hat{T}^{\star},\kappa}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hat{\mathfrak{U}}^{\star}_{\kappa}}
\scriptstyle{\hat{\mathfrak{U}}^{\star}{O}}
\textstyle{\scr{O}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{U}^{\star}{O}}
\textstyle{\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}^{\star},\kappa}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{U}_{\kappa}^{\star}}
\textstyle{\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}}

\textstyle{\scr{M}^{\hat{T}^{\star},\kappa}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hat{\mathfrak{U}}^{\star}_{\kappa}}
\scriptstyle{\hat{\mathfrak{U}}^{\star}{M}}
\textstyle{\scr{M}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{U}^{\star}{M}}
\textstyle{\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}^{\star},\kappa}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{U}_{\kappa}^{\star}}
\textstyle{\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}.}

The categories
\scr{O}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}
, respectively
\scr{O}^{\hat{T}^{\star},\kappa}_{\bullet}
are full subcategories of
\scr{M}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}
, respectively of
\scr{M}^{\hat{T}^{\star},\kappa}_{\bullet}
.

Remark 3.10
.

Exactly as already noticed in remark
2.22
, it is possible to introduce more restrictive notions of globular
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic-magma-contraction and globular
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic-contraction.

A
globular
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magma contraction
over
\bullet

(M,\pi_{M},\kappa_{M},\eta_{M},\mu_{M})
consists of a globular contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magma over
\bullet
that further satisfies the commutativity of the following two diagrams in
\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}
:

(3.25)

A
globular
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic contraction
over
\bullet
is just an globular contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operad
(M,\pi_{M},\kappa_{M},\eta_{M},\mu_{M})
that also satisfies the previous commutative diagrams.

We can introduce the category
\scr{M}\scr{K}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}
, of globular
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magma contractions over
\bullet
, as the full subcategory of
\scr{M}^{\hat{T}^{\star},\kappa}_{\bullet}
; similarly the category
\scr{O}\scr{K}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}
, of globular
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic contractions over
\bullet
, as the full subcategory of
\scr{O}^{\hat{T}^{\star},\kappa}_{\bullet}
.

\lrcorner

The following remark is absolutely crucial for us: it identifies the terminal contracted-
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operad in
\scr{O}^{\hat{T}^{\star},\kappa}_{\bullet}
.

.

\omega
-set

is naturally a globular

-collection

with projection
\pi_{\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)}
the identity morphism of globular
\omega
-sets,

for any
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection

, the projection
\pi
is a morphism in
\scr{Q}_{\bullet}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}
,

.

The (contracted) operad
\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
is final in
\scr{O}^{\hat{T}^{\star},\kappa}_{\bullet}
: for any other (contrated)
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operad
(P,\pi^{P},\mu^{P},\eta^{P},\kappa^{P})
the unique morphism of (contracted)
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operads into
\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
is given by the projection
P\xrightarrow{\pi^{P}}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
.

\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
is also a
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic contraction since it furthermore satisfies, by direct computation, the following compatibility properties between contraction and operad structures (see diagrams (

\eta_{\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)}\circ\kappa_{\pi_{\bullet}}=\kappa_{\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)}\circ\Par_{\eta_{\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)}}.
