\displaystyle\kappa_{\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)}:(\mu(x^{+}_{1},x^{+}_{2}),\mu(y_{1},y_{2}),\mu(x^{-}_{1},x^{-}_{2}))\mapsto\mu(y_{1},y_{2}),\quad\kappa_{\mu_{\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)}}:(y_{1},y_{2})\mapsto\mu(y_{1},y_{2}).

\displaystyle\kappa_{\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)}\circ^{2}_{0}\kappa_{\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)}:((x^{+}_{1},y_{1},x^{-}_{1}),(x^{+}_{1},y_{1},x^{-}_{1}))\mapsto(y_{1},y_{2}),

\displaystyle\Par_{\mu_{\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)}}:((x^{+}_{1},y_{1},x^{-}_{1}),(x^{+}_{1},y_{1},x^{-}_{1}))\mapsto(\mu(x^{+}_{1},x^{+}_{2}),\mu(y_{1},y_{2}),\mu(x^{-}_{1},x^{-}_{2})).

The
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magma contraction (respectively the
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic contraction)
\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
is final in
\scr{M}\scr{K}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}
(respectively in
\scr{O}\scr{K}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}
): the projection
M\xrightarrow{\pi_{M}}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
being the terminal morphism from any other object
M
.

\lrcorner

The following is the fundamental theorem in our paper, allowing the definition of weak involutive
\omega
-categories.

Theorem 3.12
.

The category
\scr{O}^{\hat{T}^{\star},\kappa}_{\bullet}
of globular contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operads has initial objects.

Proof.

Instead of following Leinster’s original line of proof in section
2.3.3
, we give a direct argument.

\blacktriangleright

The category
\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}
has an initial object
I
: the empty
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection
I\xrightarrow{\pi}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
given by
I^{n}:=\varnothing
, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, where all the source/target maps and the projection
\pi
are empty functions.

\blacktriangleright

Left-adjoint functors preserve colimits (see
[
Riehl 2016
, theorem 4.5.3]
) and hence they preserve initial objects (that are colimits of the empty diagram).

\blacktriangleright

Hence, if
\mathfrak{U}^{\star}{O}\circ\hat{\mathfrak{U}}^{\star}_{\kappa}=\mathfrak{U}_{\kappa}^{\star}\circ\hat{\mathfrak{U}}^{\star}{O}
has a left-adjoint
\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{L}}\scr{O}^{\hat{T}^{\star},\kappa}_{\bullet}
, the object
L^{\star}:=\mathfrak{L}^{0}(I)
is initial in
\scr{O}^{\hat{T}^{\star},\kappa}_{\bullet}
.

The theorem is now reduced to providing the existence of a
free contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operad over a
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection
. This is achieved below, in the theorem
3.16
, by an argument substantially similar to that used in our construction of the free self-dual Penon’s contractions in
[
Bejrakarbum Bertozzini 2017
, proposition 3.3]
.
∎

First we need to define suitable free structures over
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collections.
18
18

18

Of course the definitions, that for convenience are here stated for the specific case of
\hat{T}^{\star}
, work for any Cartesian monad
T
.

Definition 3.13
.

A
free globular contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operad over the
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection

Q:=Q\xrightarrow{\pi}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
consists of a contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operad
P:=(P,\pi_{P},\kappa_{P},\mu_{P},\eta_{P})
and a morphism
Q\xrightarrow{\zeta}\mathfrak{U}_{\kappa}^{\star}\circ\hat{\mathfrak{U}}{O}^{\star}(P)
in
\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}
that satisfies the following universal factorization property:
for every other morphism
Q\xrightarrow{\phi}\mathfrak{U}_{\kappa}^{\star}\circ\hat{\mathfrak{U}}{O}^{\star}(\hat{P})
in
\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}
, where
\hat{P}
is another contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operad,
there exists a unique morphism
P\xrightarrow{\hat{\phi}}\hat{P}
in
\scr{O}^{\hat{T}^{\star},\kappa}_{\bullet}
such that
\phi=\hat{\phi}\circ\zeta
.

A
free globular contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magma

M:=(M,\pi_{M},\kappa_{M},\mu_{M},\eta_{M})
over a
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection, is defined in a similar way, via a morphism
Q\xrightarrow{\xi_{Q}}\mathfrak{U}_{\kappa}^{\star}\circ\hat{\mathfrak{U}}{M}^{\star}(M)
, substituting “operads” with “operadic magmas” above.

As any universal factorization property construct, free contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operad(ic magma)s are unique, modulo a unique isomorphism compatible with the universal factorization property.
Existence is shown in theorem
3.16
.

Before embarking on the proof, we need to introduce relevant notions of
congruence
and
quotient structure
.

Definition 3.14
.

A
globular
\omega
-equivalence relation
is an equivalence relation
\mathcal{E}
in a globular
\omega
-set
Q
that is graded
19
19

19

This is equivalent to say that
\mathcal{E}
consists of a sequence
\mathcal{E}^{n}\subset Q^{n}\times Q^{n}
of equivalence relations in
Q^{n}
, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
.

\mathcal{E}\subset Q\times_{\mathbb{N}}Q:=\{(x,y)\in Q\times Q\ |\ \exists n\in{\mathbb{N}}\ :\ x,y\in Q^{n}\}
and source/target preserving:
20
20

20

In this way, the globular source and target product maps
(s,s),(t,t):Q\times_{\mathbb{N}}Q\to Q\times_{\mathbb{N}}Q
restrict to (necessarily globular) source and target maps on
{\mathscr{E}}
and hence
(\mathcal{E},(s,s)|_{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathcal{E}},(t,t)|_{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathcal{E}})
becomes a globular
\omega
-set canonically included in
Q\times_{\mathbb{N}}Q
.

(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathcal{E}\Rightarrow(s(x_{1}),s(x_{2}))\in\mathcal{E},\quad\quad(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathcal{E}\Rightarrow(t(x_{1}),t(x_{2}))\in\mathcal{E},\quad\quad\forall x,y\in Q.

(3.44)

A
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection congruence
is a globular
\omega
-equivalence relation
\mathcal{E}
in the globular
\omega
-set
Q
of a globular
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection
Q\xrightarrow{\pi}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
that is projection-preserving:

(x,y)\in\mathcal{E}\Rightarrow\pi(x)=\pi(y)
and hence
\mathcal{E}\subset Q\times_{\pi}Q
.
21
21

21

This means that the globular
\omega
-set
(\mathcal{E},(s,s),(t,t))
is a
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection
\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{\pi_{\mathcal{E}}}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
with projection
\pi_{\mathcal{E}}:(x,y)\mapsto\pi(x)=\pi(y)
; furthermore, we have a canonical inclusion morphism of
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collections
\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{\mathcal{E}}}Q\times_{\pi}Q
into the
product
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection
Q\times_{\pi}Q\xrightarrow{(\pi,\pi)}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
.

A
congruence of contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection
is a
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection congruence
\mathcal{E}
in a contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection
\Par(\pi)\xrightarrow{\kappa}Q\xrightarrow{\pi}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
that is also contraction preserving:
22
22

22

This entails that the
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection
{\mathscr{E}}\xrightarrow{\pi_{\mathcal{E}}}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
has a contraction
\kappa_{\mathcal{E}}:\Par(\pi_{\mathcal{E}})\to\mathcal{E}
given by
\kappa_{\mathcal{E}}:=(\kappa,\kappa)
.

\displaystyle\forall(x^{+}_{1},x_{2}^{+}),(x_{1}^{-},x_{2}^{-})\in\mathcal{E}\ :\ (x_{1}^{+},y_{1},x_{1}^{-}),(x_{2}^{+},y_{2},x_{2}^{-})\in\Par(\pi)\Rightarrow(\kappa(x_{1}^{+},y_{1},x_{1}^{-}),\kappa(x_{2}^{+},y_{2},x_{2}^{-}))\in\mathcal{E}.

(3.45)

A
congruence of (contracted)
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magma

(M,\mu_{M},\eta_{M})
is a congruence
\mathcal{E}
of the underlying (contracted)
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection
\Par(\pi)\xrightarrow{\kappa_{M}}M\xrightarrow{\pi_{M}}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)

that is unit and multiplication preserving:
23
23

23

The congruence
\mathcal{E}
is always unit preserving since the product morphism

\bullet\ \times_{\eta_{\hat{T}^{\star}}}\bullet\xrightarrow{(\eta_{M},\eta_{M})}M\times_{\pi_{M}}M
has always image inside
\mathcal{E}
; hence
\mathcal{E}
is equipped with a canonical unit
\bullet\xrightarrow{\eta_{\mathcal{E}}}\mathcal{E}
given by composing
\bullet\xrightarrow{!}\bullet\ \times_{\eta_{\hat{T}^{\star}}}\bullet\xrightarrow{(\eta_{M},\eta_{M})|^{\mathcal{E}}}\mathcal{E}
with isomorphism
!
of terminal objects.

\displaystyle(x,y)\in\mathcal{E}\circ^{1}_{0}\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}\times_{\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)}\hat{T}^{\star}(\mathcal{E})\quad\Rightarrow\quad\left((\mu_{M},\mu_{M})\circ\tau_{M}\circ(\varepsilon\circ^{1}_{0}\varepsilon)\right)(x,y)\in{\mathscr{E}},

(3.46)

where

\mathcal{E}\circ^{1}_{0}\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon\circ^{2}_{0}\varepsilon}(M\times_{\pi_{M}}M)\circ^{1}_{0}(M\times_{\pi_{M}}M)\xrightarrow{\tau_{M}}(M\circ^{1}_{0}M)\times_{\pi_{M}\circ^{2}_{0}\pi_{M}}(M\circ^{1}_{0}M)\xrightarrow{(\mu_{M},\mu_{M})}M\times_{\pi_{M}}M
with
\tau_{M}
denoting the canonical isomorphism of
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collections between pull-back of products and product of pull-backs in
\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}
and
!
as the unique isomorphism of terminal objects.
24
24

24

In this way, denoting by
\mathcal{E}\circ^{1}_{0}\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{\mu_{\mathcal{E}}}\mathcal{E}
the restiction
\mu_{\mathcal{E}}:=(\mu_{M},\mu_{M})\circ\tau_{M}\circ(\varepsilon\circ^{1}_{0}\varepsilon)|^{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{E}\circ^{1}_{0}\mathcal{E}}
and by
\bullet\xrightarrow{\eta_{\mathcal{E}}}\mathcal{E}

the restriction
\eta_{\mathcal{E}}:=(\eta_{M},\eta_{M})\circ!|_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{E}}
,
we have that
(\mathcal{E},\eta_{\mathcal{E}},\mu_{\mathcal{E}})
is itself a (contracted)
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magma and that the inclusion
\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}M\times_{\pi_{M}}M
is a morphisms of (contracted)
\hat{T}^{\star}
operadic magmas. Whenever
(M,\pi_{M},\kappa_{M},\eta_{M},\mu_{M})
is a (contracted)
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operad, also
(\mathcal{E},\pi_{\mathcal{E}},\kappa_{\mathcal{E}},\eta_{\mathcal{E}},\mu_{\mathcal{E}})
is.

The previous congruences have been defined, for our convenience, for 1-cells in
\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}
, but they actually work for 1-cells in the bicategory
\scr{Q}_{T}
, where
T
is any Cartesian monad on the category of globular
\omega
-sets
\scr{Q}
.

As usual, congruences produce quotients of the corresponding algebraic structures.

Proposition 3.15
.

Given a globular
\omega
-relation
\mathcal{E}\subset Q\times_{\mathbb{N}}Q
on a globular
\omega
-set
Q
, the family of quotients
Q/\mathcal{E}:=(Q^{n}/\mathcal{E}^{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
becomes a globular
\omega
-set with well-defined sources and targets
s_{Q/\mathcal{E}}([x]_{\mathcal{E}}):=[s_{Q}(x)]
,
t_{Q/\mathcal{E}}([x]_{\mathcal{E}}):=[t_{Q}(x)]_{\mathcal{E}}
; furthermore the quotient map
Q\xrightarrow{\varpi_{\mathcal{E}}}Q/\mathcal{E}
, defined as usual by
\varpi_{\mathcal{E}}:x\mapsto[x]_{\mathcal{E}}
, is a morphism of globular
\omega
-sets.

Given a congruence
\mathcal{E}\subset Q\times_{\pi}Q
of (contracted)
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection
Q\xrightarrow{\pi}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
, the quotient globular
\omega
-set
Q/\mathcal{E}
becomes a (contracted)
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection
Q/\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{\pi_{Q/\mathcal{E}}}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
with well-defined projection
\pi_{Q/\mathcal{E}}:[x]_{\mathcal{E}}\mapsto\pi(x)
(and with contraction
\kappa_{Q/\mathcal{E}}:([x^{+}]_{\mathcal{E}},y,[x^{-}]_{\mathcal{E}})\mapsto[\kappa(x^{+},y,x^{-})]_{\mathcal{E}}
on
\Par(\pi_{Q/\mathcal{E}})=\left\{([x^{+}]_{\mathcal{E}},y,[x^{-}]_{\mathcal{E}})\ |\ (x^{+},y,x^{-})\in\Par(\pi)\right\}
); furthermore the quotient map
Q\xrightarrow{\varpi_{\mathcal{E}}}Q/\mathcal{E}
is a morphism of (contracted)
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collections.

Given a congruence
\mathcal{E}\subset M\times_{\pi_{M}}M
of (contracted)
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magma on
(M,\pi_{M},\eta_{M},\mu_{M})
, the quotient (contracted)
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection
M/\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{\pi_{M/\mathcal{E}}}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
becomes a (contracted)
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magma with operadic unit
\bullet\xrightarrow{\eta_{M/\mathcal{E}}}M/\mathcal{E}
given by
\eta_{M/\mathcal{E}}:=\pi_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\eta_{M}
and
with operadic multiplication
M/\mathcal{E}\circ^{1}_{0}M/\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{\mu_{M/\mathcal{E}}}M/\mathcal{E}
that is well-defined by

\mu_{M/\mathcal{E}}:=([x]_{\mathcal{E}},[y]_{\mathcal{E}})\mapsto[\mu_{M}(x,y)]_{\mathcal{E}}
, for all
(x,y)\in M\circ^{1}_{0}M=M\times_{\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)}\hat{T}^{\star}(M)
; furthermore the quotient map
M\xrightarrow{\varpi_{\mathcal{E}}}M/\mathcal{E}
is a morphism of (contracted)
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magmas.

Given a morphism
Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\phi}Q_{2}
of the categories in proposition
3.9
, a congruence
\mathcal{E}
of the respective type in
Q_{2}
naturally induces a congruence, of the same type,
\mathcal{E}_{\phi}:=\big\{(x,y)\in Q_{1}\times Q_{1}\ |\ (\phi(x),\phi(y))\in\mathcal{E}\big\}
in
Q_{1}
.
Furthermore if
\mathcal{E}_{1}
is a congruence of the respective type in
Q_{1}
such that
\mathcal{E}_{1}\subset\mathcal{E}_{\phi}
, there exists a unique well-defined quotient morphism
Q_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}\xrightarrow{\hat{\phi}}Q_{2}
given by
\hat{\phi}:[x]_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\mapsto\phi(x)
such that
\phi=\hat{\phi}\circ\pi_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}
.

Proof.

By property (
3.44
), for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, the source and target
s^{n}_{Q/\mathcal{E}},t^{n}_{Q/\mathcal{E}}:Q^{n+1}/\mathcal{E}^{n+1}\to Q^{n}/\mathcal{E}^{n}
are well-defined and their globularity property follows from the globularity of
Q
.
For all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, the quotient map
\varpi^{n}_{\mathcal{E}}:Q^{n}\to Q^{n}/\mathcal{E}^{n}
is well-defined by
x\mapsto[x]_{\mathcal{E}^{n}}
; and
\varpi_{\mathcal{E}}:=(\pi^{n}_{\mathcal{E}})_{\mathbb{N}}:Q\to Q/\mathcal{E}
becomes a morphism in
\scr{Q}
since

s^{n}_{Q/\mathcal{E}}(\varpi^{n+1}_{\mathcal{E}}(x))=s^{n}_{Q/\mathcal{E}}([x]_{\mathcal{E}^{n+1}})=[s^{n}_{Q}(x)]_{\mathcal{E}^{n}}=\varpi^{n}_{\mathcal{E}}(s_{Q}^{n}(x))
, for all
x\in Q^{n+1}
and similarly for targets.

A congruence
\mathcal{E}\subset Q\times_{\pi}Q
of a collection
Q\xrightarrow{\pi}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
is necessarily a congruence of
\omega
-globular sets, hence we already have a quotient morphism
Q\xrightarrow{\varpi_{\mathcal{E}}}Q/\mathcal{E}
in
\scr{Q}
. Since
\mathcal{E}\subset Q\times_{\pi}Q
, the assignment
[x]_{\mathcal{E}}\mapsto\pi(x)
is a well-defined graded function
\pi_{Q/\mathcal{E}}:Q/\mathcal{E}\to\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
that is actually a morphism of globular
\omega
-sets in
\scr{Q}
: for all
[x]_{\mathcal{E}}\in Q/\mathcal{E}
,
\pi_{Q/\mathcal{E}}(s_{Q/\mathcal{E}}[x]_{\mathcal{E}})=\pi_{Q/\mathcal{E}}([s_{Q}(x)]_{\mathscr{E}})=\pi(s_{Q}(x))=s(\pi(x))=s_{\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)}(\pi_{Q/\mathcal{E}}[x]_{\mathcal{E}})
and similarly for targets.
Since
\pi_{Q/\mathcal{E}}\circ\varpi_{\mathcal{E}}(x)=\pi_{Q/\mathcal{E}}([x]_{\mathcal{E}})=\pi(x)
, for all
x\in Q
, the quotient
Q\xrightarrow{\varpi_{\mathcal{E}}}Q/\mathcal{E}
is a morphism of
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collections in
\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}.

Suppose now that
\Par(\pi)\xrightarrow{\kappa}Q\xrightarrow{\pi}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
is a contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection and
\mathcal{E}\subset Q\times_{\pi}Q
is a congruence of contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collections. For the quotient
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection
Q/\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{\pi_{Q/\mathcal{E}}}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
we have that
[x_{1}]_{\mathcal{E}},[x_{2}]_{\mathcal{E}}\in Q/\mathcal{E}
are
\pi_{Q/\mathcal{E}}
-parallel if and only if
x_{1},x_{2}
are
\pi
-parallel and hence
\Par(\pi_{Q/\mathcal{E}})=\big\{([x^{+}]_{\mathcal{E}},y,[x^{-}]_{\mathcal{E}})\ |\ (x^{+},y,x^{-})\in\Par(\pi)\big\}
.
Furthermore, since
\Par_{\pi_{Q/\mathcal{E}}}:(x^{+},y,x^{-})\mapsto([x^{+}],y,[x^{-}])
there exists a unique relation
\Par(\pi_{Q/\mathcal{E}})\xrightarrow{\kappa_{Q/\mathcal{E}}}Q/\mathcal{E}
that satisfies
\Par_{\pi_{\mathcal{E}}}\circ\kappa_{Q/\mathcal{E}}=\kappa
and that is given by
\kappa_{Q/\mathcal{E}}([x^{+}]_{\mathcal{E}},y,[x^{-}]_{\mathcal{E}}):=[\kappa(x^{+},y,x^{-})]_{\mathcal{E}}
.
Since
\mathcal{E}
is a congruence of contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collections, from equation (
3.45
) we see that
\kappa_{Q/\mathcal{E}}
is actually a well-defined function and hence a contraction on
Q/\mathcal{E}
and
Q\xrightarrow{\varpi_{\mathcal{E}}}Q/\mathcal{E}
is a morphism in
\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}^{\star},\kappa}_{\bullet}
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a congruence of the (contracted) operadic magma
(M,\pi_{M},\kappa_{M},\eta_{M},\mu_{M})
. We already know that the quotient
(M/\mathcal{E},\pi_{M/\mathcal{E}},\kappa_{M/\mathcal{E}})
is a (contracted)
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection and that
M\xrightarrow{\varpi_{\mathcal{E}}}M/\mathcal{E}
is a morphism in
\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}^{\star},\kappa}_{\bullet}
.

The operadic unit map
\eta_{M/\mathcal{E}}:\bullet\mapsto[\eta_{M}(\bullet)]_{\mathcal{E}}
is well defined, and we immediately get
\eta_{M/\mathcal{E}}=\pi_{M/\mathcal{E}}\circ\eta_{M}
, hence the quotient morphism
\pi_{M/\mathcal{E}}
preserves units.

To describe the operadic multiplication, we first notice that we have a canonical exchange isomorphism
(M/\mathcal{E})\circ^{1}_{0}(M/\mathcal{E})\xrightarrow{\chi}(M\circ^{1}_{0}M)/(\mathcal{E}\circ^{1}_{0}\mathcal{E})
; any multiplication morphism
(M/\mathcal{E})\circ^{1}_{0}(M/\mathcal{E})\xrightarrow{\mu_{M/\mathcal{E}}}M/\mathcal{E}
such that
\mu_{M/\mathcal{E}}\circ(\varpi_{\mathcal{E}}\circ^{2}_{0}\varpi_{E})=\varpi_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\mu_{M}
, must necessarily be given by
\mu_{M/\mathcal{E}}:=\hat{\mu}_{M/\mathcal{E}}\circ\chi
where, using the congruence property (
3.46
) of
\mathcal{E}
, we have that
\hat{\mu}_{M/\mathcal{E}}:[(x,y)]_{\mathcal{E}\circ^{1}_{0}\mathcal{E}}\mapsto\varpi_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\mu_{M}(x,y)
, for
(x,y)\in M\circ^{1}_{0}M
, is a well-defined morphism of (contracted)
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collections.
Since
\mu_{M/\mathcal{E}}\circ(\varpi_{\mathcal{E}}\circ^{2}_{0}\varpi_{E})=\hat{\mu}_{M/\mathcal{E}}\circ\chi\circ(\varpi_{\mathcal{E}}\circ^{2}_{0}\varpi_{E})=\hat{\mu}_{M/\mathcal{E}}\circ\varpi_{\mathcal{E}\circ^{1}_{0}\mathcal{E}}=\varpi_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\mu_{M}
, we see that
\pi_{\mathcal{E}}
is a morphism of (contracted)
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magmas.

The family
\mathcal{E}_{\phi}\subset Q_{1}\times Q_{1}
is an equivalence relation in
Q_{1}
and, since
\phi
is grade-preserving, we also have
\mathcal{E}_{\phi}\subset Q_{1}\times_{\mathbb{N}}Q_{1}
and hence
\mathcal{E}_{\phi}
consists of a family of equivalence relations
\mathcal{E}_{\phi}^{n}\subset Q_{1}^{n}\times Q_{1}^{n}
, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Since
\phi
is always a morphism of globular
\omega
-sets,
s_{Q_{2}}^{n}\circ\phi^{n+1}=\phi^{n}\circ s_{Q_{1}}^{n}
(and similarly for targets), for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, and hence property (
3.44
) holds and
\mathcal{E}_{\phi}
is a globular
\omega
-equivalence relation in
Q_{1}
.

If
Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\phi}Q_{2}
is a morphism of
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collections in
\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}
,
(x,y)\in\mathcal{E}_{\phi}\Rightarrow\pi_{Q_{2}}(\phi(x))=\pi_{Q_{2}}(\phi(y))\Rightarrow\pi_{Q_{1}}(x)=\pi_{Q_{1}}(y)
and hence
\mathcal{E}_{\phi}\subset Q_{1}\times_{\pi_{Q_{1}}}Q_{1}
is a congruence of
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collections in
Q_{1}
.

Whenever
\phi
is a morphism of contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collections in
\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}^{\star},\kappa}_{\bullet}
, given
(x^{+}_{1},y_{1},x^{-}_{1}),(x^{+}_{2},y_{2},x^{-}_{2})\in\Par(\pi_{1})
with
(x^{+}_{1},x^{+}_{2}),(x^{-}_{1},x^{-}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\phi}
, we must show that
(\kappa_{1}(x^{+}_{1},y_{1},x^{-}_{1}),\kappa_{1}(x^{+}_{2},y_{2},x^{-}_{2}))\in\mathcal{E}_{\phi}
that is equivalent to show

(\kappa_{2}\circ\Par_{\phi}(x^{+}_{1},y_{1},x^{-}_{1}),\kappa_{2}\circ\Par(x^{+}_{1},y_{1},x^{-}_{1}))=(\phi\circ\kappa_{1}(x^{+}_{1},y_{1},x^{-}_{1}),\phi\circ\kappa_{1}(x^{+}_{1},y_{1},x^{-}_{1}))\in\mathcal{E}
and this is true since we have
(\phi(x_{1}^{+}),y_{1},\phi(x_{1}^{-})),(\phi(x_{2}^{+}),y_{2},\phi(x_{2}^{-}))\in\Par(\pi_{2})
and
(\phi(x_{1}^{+}),\phi(x_{2}^{+})),(\phi(x_{1}^{-}),\phi(x_{2}^{-}))\in\mathcal{E}
and hence also

(k_{2}(\phi(x_{1})^{+},y_{1},\phi(x_{1})^{-}),\kappa_{2}(\phi(x_{2})^{+},y_{2},\phi(x_{2})^{-}))=(\kappa_{2}\circ\Par_{\phi}(x^{+}_{1},y_{1},x^{-}_{1}),\kappa_{2}\circ\Par(x^{+}_{1},y_{1},x^{-}_{1}))\in\mathcal{E}
.
This shows that
\mathcal{E}_{\phi}
is also a congruence of contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection in
Q_{1}
.

Finally if
\mathcal{E}
is a congruence of (contracted)
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magma in
Q_{2}
and
Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\phi}Q_{2}
is a morphism in
\scr{M}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}
:

(x,y)\in\mathcal{E}_{\phi}\circ^{1}_{0}\mathcal{E}_{\phi}\Rightarrow(\phi\circ^{2}_{0}\phi)(x,y)\in\mathcal{E}\circ^{1}_{0}\mathcal{E}\Rightarrow\mu_{\mathcal{E}}\circ(\phi\circ^{2}_{0}\phi)(x,y)\in\mathcal{E}\Rightarrow(\phi\circ^{2}_{0}\phi)(\mu_{\mathcal{E}}(x,y))\in\mathcal{E}\Rightarrow\mu_{\mathcal{E}}(x,y)\in\mathcal{E}_{\phi}
, hence
\mathcal{E}_{\phi}
is a congruence
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magmas. The same argument assures that, if
\phi
is a morphism in
\scr{M}^{\hat{T}^{\star},\kappa}_{\bullet}
,
\mathcal{E}_{\phi}
is a congruence of contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magmas.

Finally, whenever
\mathcal{E}_{1}\subset\mathcal{E}_{\phi}
is a congruence (of the “same type” of the morphism
\phi
) in
Q_{1}
, any relation
Q_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}\xrightarrow{\hat{\phi}}Q_{2}
such that
\phi=\hat{\phi}\circ\pi_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}
must necessarily associate
[x]_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\mapsto\phi(x)
and this is a well defined function since
[x]_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\subset[x]_{\mathcal{E}_{\phi}}
and hence
\phi(x)
does not depend on the representative element.

We must show that
Q_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}\xrightarrow{\hat{\phi}}Q_{2}
is a morphism in the same category of
\phi
.

From its definition
\hat{\phi}
is already a graded map:
\hat{\phi}(Q_{1}^{n}/\mathcal{E}^{n}_{1})\subset Q_{2}^{n}
, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
. For all
x\in Q_{1}
, we have
s_{Q_{2}}(\hat{\phi}([x]_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}))=s_{Q_{2}}(\phi(x))=\phi(s_{Q_{1}}(x))=\hat{\phi}([s_{Q_{1}}(x)]_{\mathcal{E}_{1}})=\hat{\phi}(s_{Q_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}}([x]_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}))
and similarly for the target; hence
\hat{\phi}
is a morphism in
\scr{Q}
.
Since
\pi_{Q_{2}}(\hat{\phi}([x]_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}))=\pi_{Q_{2}}(\phi(x))=\pi_{Q_{1}}(x)=\pi_{Q_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}}([x]_{\mathcal{E}_{1}})
, for all
x\in Q_{1}
, we also have that
\hat{\phi}
is a morphism in
\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}
.
Since
\hat{\phi}
is already a morphism of
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collections it naturally induces a map
\Par_{\hat{\phi}}:\Par(\pi_{Q_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}})\to\Par(\pi_{Q_{2}})
and, whenever
\phi
is a morphism in
\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}^{\star},\kappa}
, we show that
\kappa_{Q_{2}}\circ\Par_{\hat{\phi}}=\hat{\phi}\circ\kappa_{Q_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}}
:

\displaystyle\kappa_{Q_{2}}(\Par_{\hat{\phi}}([x^{+}]_{\mathcal{E}_{1}},y,[x^{-}]_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}))

\displaystyle=\kappa_{Q_{2}}(\Par_{\hat{\phi}}\circ\Par_{\pi_{\frac{Q_{1}}{\mathcal{E}_{1}}}}(x^{+},y,x^{-}))=\kappa_{Q_{2}}(\Par_{\phi}(x^{+},y,x^{-}))

\displaystyle=\phi(\kappa_{Q_{1}}(x^{+},y,x^{-}))=\hat{\phi}(\pi_{\frac{Q_{1}}{\mathcal{E}_{1}}}(x^{+},y,x^{-}))=\hat{\phi}(\kappa_{\frac{Q_{1}}{\mathcal{E}_{1}}}([x^{+}]_{\mathcal{E}_{1}},y,[x^{-}]_{\mathcal{E}_{1}})),

for all
(x^{+},y,x^{-})\in\Par(\pi_{Q_{1}})
, and hence
\hat{\phi}
is a morphism in
\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}
.

Supposing now that
Q_{1}\xrightarrow{\phi}Q_{2}
is a morphism in
\scr{M}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}
and
\mathcal{E}_{1}
is a congruence of
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magmas, we have
\hat{\phi}\circ\eta_{Q_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}}=\hat{\phi}\circ\pi_{Q_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}}\circ\eta_{Q_{1}}=\phi\circ\eta_{Q_{1}}=\eta_{Q_{2}}
.
As regards multiplication, from
\phi=\hat{\phi}\circ\pi_{Q_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}}
we obtain
(\phi\circ^{2}_{0}\phi)=(\hat{\phi}\circ^{2}_{0}\hat{\phi})\circ(\pi_{Q_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}}\circ^{2}_{0}\pi_{Q_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}})
; since
\mu_{Q_{2}}\circ(\phi\circ^{2}_{0}\phi)=\phi\circ\mu_{Q_{1}}
and
\mu_{Q_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}}\circ(\pi_{Q_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}}\circ^{2}_{0}\pi_{Q_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}})=\pi_{Q_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}}\circ\mu_{Q_{1}}
, we get:

\mu_{Q_{2}}\circ(\hat{\phi}\circ^{2}_{0}\hat{\phi})\circ(\pi_{Q_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}}\circ^{2}_{0}\pi_{Q_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}})=\mu_{Q_{2}}\circ(\phi\circ^{2}_{0}\phi)=\phi\circ\mu_{Q_{1}}=\hat{\phi}\circ\pi_{Q_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}}\circ\mu_{Q_{1}}=\hat{\phi}\circ\mu_{Q_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}}\circ(\pi_{Q_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}}\circ^{2}_{0}\pi_{Q_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}})
. Since
(\pi_{Q_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}}\circ^{2}_{0}\pi_{Q_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}})
is an epimorphism, we finally have

\mu_{Q_{2}}\circ(\hat{\phi}\circ^{2}_{0}\hat{\phi})=\hat{\phi}\circ\mu_{Q_{1}/\mathcal{E}_{1}}
and hence
\hat{\phi}
is a morphism of (contracted)
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magmas.
∎

Theorem 3.16
.

A free contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operad over a
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection always exists.

Proof.

We proceed with a low-tech quite long, but direct, iterative construction followed by a quotient.

a.

starting from a globular
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection
Q\xrightarrow{\pi_{Q}}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
a new globular
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection
\mathfrak{M}(Q)\xrightarrow{\pi_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
is inductively constructed together with a morphism
\xi_{Q}:Q\to\mathfrak{M}(Q)
of globular
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collections;

b.

we show that
\mathfrak{M}(Q)\xrightarrow{\pi_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
can be equipped with a contraction
\kappa_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}:\Par(\pi_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)})\to\mathfrak{M}(Q)
,
a unit
\eta_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}:\bullet\to\mathfrak{M}(Q)
and a multiplication
\mu_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}:\mathfrak{M}(Q)\circ^{1}_{0}\mathfrak{M}(Q)=\mathfrak{M}(Q)\times_{\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)}\hat{T}^{\star}(\mathfrak{M}(Q))\to\mathfrak{M}(Q)
;

c.

it is shown that
Q\xrightarrow{\xi_{Q}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)
is a free contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic-magma over
Q\xrightarrow{\pi_{Q}}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
;

d.

we establish the existence of the smallest congruence
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}
generated by operadic associativity and unitality axioms on the contracted operadic
\hat{T}^{\star}
-magma
\mathfrak{M}(Q)
, so that the quotient
\mathfrak{M}(Q)\xrightarrow{\pi_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}}}\mathfrak{P}(Q):=\mathfrak{M}(Q)/\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}
becomes a morphism of contracted operadic
\hat{T}^{\star}
-magmas onto a contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operad
\mathfrak{P}(Q)\xrightarrow{\pi_{\mathfrak{P}(Q)}}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
with
operadic unit
\eta_{\mathfrak{P}(Q)}:\bullet\to\mathfrak{P}(Q)
operadic multiplication
\mu_{\mathfrak{P}(Q)}:\mathfrak{P}(Q)\circ^{1}_{0}\mathfrak{P}(Q)\to\mathfrak{P}(Q)
and contraction
\kappa_{\mathfrak{P}(Q)}:\Par(\pi_{\mathfrak{P}(Q)})\to\mathfrak{P}(Q)
, as explained in proposition
3.15
;

e.

the universal factorization property for free contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operads is proved for
Q\xrightarrow{\zeta_{Q}:=\pi_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}}\circ\xi_{Q}}\mathfrak{P}(Q)
.

a.

We start by explicitly providing
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{j}\xrightarrow{\pi_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}^{j}}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)^{j}
, for
j=0,1
.
Define
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{0}:=Q^{0}\biguplus Q_{\eta}^{0}
, disjoint union of
Q^{0}
and a singleton
Q^{0}_{\eta}:=\{\bullet^{0}_{\eta}\}
, and
\pi_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}^{0}:\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{0}\to\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)^{0}=\{\bullet^{0}\}
as the terminal map, necessarily coinciding with the terminal map
\pi_{Q}^{0}
on
Q^{0}
and given by
\bullet^{0}_{\eta}\mapsto\bullet^{0}
on the singleton.
Notice that, at this level-
0
stage, no contraction-cells or operadic multiplication-cells are added, but only a “free operadic unity-cell”
\bullet^{0}_{\eta}
.

Passing to the level-
1
, we define
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[0]:=Q^{1}\biguplus Q^{1}_{\eta}\biguplus Q^{1}_{\kappa}
,
where
Q^{1}_{\eta}:=\{\bullet^{1}_{\eta}\}
is again a singleton (corresponding to a free operadic-unit 1-cell);
Q^{1}_{\kappa}:=\Par(\pi^{0}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)})
consists of a copy of the set of free contraction 1-cells for the parallel
0
-cells induced by the projection
\pi_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}^{0}
at the previous 0-level;
we set the map
\pi_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}^{1}[0]:\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[0]\to\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)^{1}
coinciding with
\pi_{Q}^{1}
on
Q^{1}
, as a terminal map
\bullet^{1}_{\eta}\mapsto\bullet^{1}\in\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)^{1}
on
Q^{1}_{\eta}
, and as the map
(x^{+},y,x^{-})\mapsto y
on all
(x^{+},y,x^{-})\in Q^{1}_{\kappa}
; furthermore we introduce new source/target maps
s_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}^{0}[0],t_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}^{0}[0]:\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[0]\to\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{0}
coinciding with the original source/target maps on
Q^{1}
; as
\bullet^{1}_{\eta}\mapsto\bullet^{0}_{\eta}
on
Q^{1}_{\eta}
; and as
s_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}^{0}[0]:(x^{+},y,x^{-})\mapsto x^{-}
, respectively
t_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}^{0}[0]:(x^{+},y,x^{-})\mapsto x^{+}
on
Q^{1}_{\kappa}
.

Finally we introduce additional free operadic multiplication-1-cells by
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}:=\biguplus_{k=0}^{+\infty}\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[k]
, where we inductively have
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[k+1]:=\Big\{(x,\mu,y)\ |\ (x,y)\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[k_{1}]\times_{\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)}\hat{T}^{\star}(\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[k_{2}]),\ k_{1}+k_{2}=k\Big\}
;
we recursively define the projection maps
\pi^{1}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}[k+1]:(x,\mu,y)\mapsto\pi^{1}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}[k](x)=\mu^{\star}_{\bullet}\left((\hat{T}^{\star}(\pi^{1}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}[k]))(y)\right)
, targets
t^{0}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}[k+1]:(x,\mu,y)\mapsto(t^{0}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}[k])(x)
and sources

s^{0}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}[k+1]:(x,\mu,y)\mapsto\mu^{\star}_{P}\left((\hat{T}^{\star}(s^{0}[k]))(y)\right)
, for all

(x,y)\in\mathfrak{P}(Q)^{1}[k]\times_{\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)}\hat{T}^{\star}(\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{1}[k])
.

Assuming now inductively the existence of
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\xrightarrow{\pi_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}^{n}}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)^{n}
, we construct
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}\xrightarrow{\pi_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}^{n+1}}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)^{n+1}
: starting from
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}[0]:=Q^{n+1}\biguplus Q^{n+1}_{\eta}\biguplus Q^{n+1}_{\kappa}
, where

Q^{n+1}_{\eta}:=\{\bullet^{n+1}_{\eta}\}
is a singleton free operadic-unit
(n+1)
-cell
and

Q^{n+1}_{\kappa}:=\Par(\pi^{n}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)})
is a family of free contraction
(n+1)
-cells induced by the already defined projection map
\pi^{n}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}:\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}\to\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
,
we introduce free operadic-multiplication
(n+1)
-cells by the recursive nesting
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}[k+1]:=\Big\{(x,\mu,y)\ |\ (x,y)\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}[k_{1}]\times_{\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)}\hat{T}^{\star}(\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}[k_{2}]),\ k_{1}+k_{2}=k\Big\}
and we get
\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}:=\biguplus_{k=0}^{+\infty}\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}[k]
.
The projection map
\pi^{n+1}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}
is separately defined on each set of the disjoint union: it coincides with
\pi^{n+1}_{Q}
on
Q^{n+1}\subset\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}[0]
; it is
\bullet_{\eta}^{n+1}\mapsto\bullet^{n+1}
on
Q^{n+1}_{\eta}
; it is
(x^{+},y,x^{-})\mapsto y
on
Q^{n+1}_{\kappa}
;
and it is recursively given
by
\pi_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}^{n+1}:(x,\mu,y)\mapsto\pi^{n}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}[k](x)=\mu_{\hat{T}^{\star}}\left(\hat{T}^{\star}(\pi^{n}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}[k])(y)\right)

on the elements
(x,\mu,y)\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}[k+1]
.
Finally we obtain a globular
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collection with target/source maps given by
t^{n+1}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}[k+1]:(x,\mu,y)\mapsto(t^{n+1}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}[k])(x)
and
s^{n+1}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}[k+1](x,\mu,y):(x,\mu,y)\mapsto\mu^{\star}_{P}\left((\hat{T}^{\star}(s^{n+1}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}[k]))(y)\right)
, where
(x,y)\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}[k]\times_{\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)}\hat{T}^{\star}(\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}[k])
.

The inclusions
\xi_{Q}^{n}:Q^{n}\to Q^{n}\biguplus Q^{n}_{\eta}\biguplus Q^{n}_{\kappa}=\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}[0]\subset\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}=\biguplus_{k=0}^{+\infty}\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}[k]
, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
define level-by-level the map
\xi_{Q}:Q\to\mathfrak{M}(Q)
that is already a morphism of globular
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collections.

b.

We construct level-by-level the several structural maps involved in the definition of contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magma: the contraction
\kappa_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}
, the unit
\eta_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}
, the multiplication
\mu_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}
.

The operadic unit
\eta_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}:\bullet\to\mathfrak{M}(Q)
is defined as
\eta^{n}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}:\bullet^{n}\mapsto\bullet_{\eta}^{n}
, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
.

The operadic multiplication

\mu_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}:\mathfrak{M}(Q)\times_{\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)}\hat{T}^{\star}(\mathfrak{M}(Q))\to\mathfrak{M}(Q)
is given, at each level
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, for all
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
, by the maps

\mu^{n}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}[k]:\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}[k]\times_{\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)}\hat{T}^{\star}(\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}[k])\to\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}[k+1]
given as
\mu^{n}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}[k]:(x,y)\mapsto(x,\mu,y)
.

The contraction
\kappa_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}:\Par(\pi_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)})\to\mathfrak{M}(Q)
is provided by the maps
\kappa^{n}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}:\Par(\pi^{n}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)})\to\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}
defined, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, as inclusions
\kappa^{n}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}:Q_{\kappa}^{n}\to\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n+1}
.

c.

Here we deal with the universal factorization property of
Q\xrightarrow{\xi_{Q}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)
:
given another contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magma
\hat{M}\xrightarrow{\hat{\pi}}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
with contraction
\hat{\kappa}:\Par(\hat{\pi})\to\hat{M}
, operadic unit
\hat{\eta}:\bullet\to\hat{M}
and operadic multiplication
\hat{\mu}:\hat{M}\times_{\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)}\hat{T}^{\star}(\hat{M})\to\hat{M}
, we show the existence of a unique morphism
\hat{\phi}:\mathfrak{M}(Q)\to\hat{M}
of contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magmas such that
\phi=\hat{\phi}\circ\xi_{Q}
.

Since, by construction, the inclusion
Q\xrightarrow{\xi_{Q}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)
, maps every element
x\in Q^{n}
to the same element
x\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}
, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, we must necessarily have that
\hat{\phi}(x):=x
, for all
x\in Q\subset\mathfrak{M}(Q)
.
Since
\hat{\phi}
should preserve the unit,
\hat{\phi}\circ\eta_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}=\hat{\eta}
, the explicit construction of
\eta_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}
, necessarily entails
\hat{\phi}:\eta_{\mathfrak{M}}(\bullet)\mapsto\hat{\eta}(\bullet)
, for all the elements
\eta_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(\bullet)\subset\mathfrak{M}(Q)
.
Similarly, since
\hat{\phi}
should be contraction preserving,
\hat{\phi}\circ\kappa_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}=\hat{\kappa}\circ(\hat{\phi},\hat{\phi})
, the only possible choice for the restriction of
\hat{\phi}
on
\kappa_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(\Par(\pi_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}))\subset\mathfrak{M}(Q)
is:

\hat{\phi}(x^{+},y,x^{-})\mapsto\hat{\kappa}(\hat{\phi}(x^{+}),y,\hat{\phi}(x^{-}))
.
25
25

25

Notice that, if
(x^{+},y,x^{-})\in\Par(\pi_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)})^{n}
, we have
x^{+},x^{-}\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n-1}
and, since the construction of
(\mathfrak{M}(Q),\pi_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)},\kappa_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)})
, is produced inductively, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, the definition of
\hat{\phi}
on the elements
\kappa_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(\Par(\pi_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}))^{n}\subset\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}
requires only the knowledge of the already available map
\hat{\phi}^{n-1}
.

Since
\hat{\phi}
should preserve multiplications
\hat{\phi}^{n}\circ(\mu_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)})^{n}=\hat{\mu}^{n}\circ(\hat{\phi}^{n},(\hat{T}^{\star}(\hat{\phi}))^{n})
, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, the map
\hat{\phi}^{n}
should be uniquely defined as
(x,\mu,y)\mapsto\hat{\mu}(\hat{\phi}^{n},\hat{T}^{\star}(\hat{\phi})^{n}(y))
on the elements in
\mu_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}\left(\mathfrak{M}(Q)\times_{\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)}\hat{T}^{\star}(\mathfrak{M}(Q))^{n}\right)\subset\mathfrak{M}(Q)^{n}
.
26
26

26

Notice again that, because of the inductive definition of
\mathfrak{M}(Q)
, the elements in
\mathfrak{M}(Q)\times_{\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)}\hat{T}^{\star}(\mathfrak{M}(Q))^{n}
only require the knowledge of already defined
\hat{\phi}^{k}
, for all
k\leq n
on .

The already uniquely constructed
\hat{\phi}:\mathfrak{M}(Q)\to\hat{M}
is a morphism of globular contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magmas that also satisfies
\phi=\hat{\phi}\circ\xi_{Q}
.

d.

The previously constructed free contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magma
\mathfrak{M}(Q)
is not yet a free contracted operad because its free unit
\eta_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}:\bullet\to\mathfrak{M}(Q)
and free multiplication
\mu_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}:\mathfrak{M}(Q)\circ^{1}_{0}\mathfrak{M}(Q)\to\mathfrak{M}(Q)
fail to satisfy the unitality and associativity axioms for a monad in the bicategory
\scr{Q}_{\hat{T}^{\star}}
as specified by the commuting diagrams in definition
2.6
, in detail, denoting
the associators and left/right unitors morphisms by

(\mathfrak{M}(Q)\circ^{1}_{0}\mathfrak{M}(Q))\circ_{0}^{1}\mathfrak{M}(Q)\xrightarrow{\alpha_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)\circ^{1}_{0}(\mathfrak{M}(Q)\circ_{0}^{1}\mathfrak{M}(Q))
and
\iota^{1}(\bullet)\circ^{1}_{0}\mathfrak{M}(Q)\xrightarrow{\lambda_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)\xleftarrow{\rho_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)\circ^{1}_{0}\iota^{1}(\bullet)
, we need to get identified all the pairs of terms in
\mathfrak{M}(Q)
contained in the following family
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathfrak{M}(Q)\times\mathfrak{M}(Q)
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}

\displaystyle:=\Big\{\left(\lambda_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x_{1})\ ,\ \mu_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}\left((\eta_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}\circ^{2}_{0}\iota^{1}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)})(x_{1})\right)\right)\ \big|\ x_{1}\in\iota^{1}(\bullet)\circ^{1}_{0}\mathfrak{M}(Q)\Big\}\ \cup

\displaystyle\Big\{\left(\rho_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x_{2})\ ,\ \mu_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}\left((\iota^{1}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}\circ^{2}_{0}\eta_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)})(x_{2})\right)\right)\ \big|\ x_{2}\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)\circ^{1}_{0}\iota^{1}(\bullet)\Big\}\ \cup

(3.47)

\displaystyle\Big\{\left(\mu_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}((\iota^{1}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}\circ^{2}_{0}\mu_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)})\circ\alpha_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x_{3}))\ ,\ \mu_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}((\mu_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}\circ^{2}_{0}\iota^{1}_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)})(x_{3}))\right)\ \big|\ x_{3}\in(\mathfrak{M}(Q)\circ^{1}_{0}\mathfrak{M}(Q))\circ_{0}^{1}\mathfrak{M}(Q)\Big\}.

To solve this problem, we define
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}
as the
smallest congruence of contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magma
in
\mathfrak{M}(Q)
containing all the pairs of terms in
\mathcal{X}
.
From remark
3.11

\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)\xrightarrow{\pi_{\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)}}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
is a contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operad and, since
\mathfrak{M}(Q)\xrightarrow{\pi_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
is morphism of contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magmas, by proposition
3.15
we know that the canonical equivalence relation
\mathcal{E}_{\pi_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}}:=\mathfrak{M}(Q)\times_{\pi_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)=\left\{(x,y)\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)\times\mathfrak{M}(Q)\ |\ \pi_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(x)=\pi_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}(y)\right\}
is itself a congruence of contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magmas with
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathcal{E}_{\pi_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}}
and hence
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}
can be taken as the intersection of the (non-empty) family of all such congruences containing
\mathcal{X}
in
\mathfrak{M}(Q)
.

By proposition
3.15
, the quotient map
\mathfrak{M}(Q)\xrightarrow{\pi_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}}}\mathfrak{P}(Q):=\mathfrak{M}(Q)/\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}
is a morphism of contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magmas and, since
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}
, the quotient
\mathfrak{P}(Q):=\mathfrak{M}(Q)/\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\xrightarrow{\pi_{\mathfrak{P}(Q)}}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
, where
\pi_{\mathfrak{P}(Q)}
is the only morphism of contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magmas such that
\pi_{\mathfrak{M}(Q)}=\pi_{\mathfrak{P}(Q)}\circ\pi_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}}
, is not only a contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magma, but it is already a contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operad.

The inclusion
Q\xrightarrow{\zeta_{Q}}\mathfrak{P}(Q)
given by
\zeta_{Q}:=\pi_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}}\circ\xi_{Q}
is, by composition, a morphism of globular
\omega
-sets.

e.

Suppose that
Q\xrightarrow{\phi}\hat{P}
is a morphism of
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collections into another contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operad
\hat{P}\xrightarrow{\hat{\pi}_{P}}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
with contraction
\hat{\kappa}_{P}:\Par(\hat{\pi}_{P})\to\hat{P}
, operadic unit
\hat{\eta}_{P}:\bullet\to\hat{P}
and operadic multiplication
\hat{\mu}_{P}:\hat{P}\circ^{1}_{0}\hat{P}\to\hat{P}
.

Since (forgetting the associativity and unitality axioms) every contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operad is a contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magma, by the previous point c. above, there exists a unique morphism
\mathfrak{M}(Q)\xrightarrow{\tilde{\phi}}\hat{P}
of contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magmas, defined on the free contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magma
\mathfrak{M}(Q)
, such that
\phi=\tilde{\phi}\circ\xi_{Q}
.

The equivalence relation
\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{\phi}}:=\mathfrak{M}(Q)\times_{\tilde{\phi}}\mathfrak{M}(Q)=\{(x,y)\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)\times\mathfrak{M}(Q)\ |\ \tilde{\phi}(x)=\tilde{\phi}(y)\}
induced by the morphism
\mathfrak{M}(Q)\xrightarrow{\tilde{\phi}}\hat{P}
into the contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operad
\hat{P}
, is a congruence of contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic magma that contains all the terms
\mathcal{X}
generating
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}
and hence, by the minimality of
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}
, we have
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\subset\mathcal{E}_{\tilde{\phi}}
. It follows, by proposition
3.15
, that there exists a unique quotient morphism
\mathfrak{P}(Q)\xrightarrow{\hat{\phi}}\hat{P}
of contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operads, given by
\hat{\phi}(\pi_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}}(x)):=\tilde{\phi}(x)
, for all
x\in\mathfrak{M}(Q)
.
The universal factorization property for free
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operads over
Q
is satisfied since:

\hat{\phi}\circ\zeta_{Q}=\hat{\phi}\circ\pi_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{X}}}\circ\xi_{Q}=\tilde{\phi}\circ\xi_{Q}=\phi
.
∎

Remark 3.17
.

The proof of theorem
3.16
could be obtained mimicking Leinster’s techniques in section
2.3.3
. Specifically, applying lemma
2.28

after showing that:
a) the category
\scr{Q}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}
is locally finitely presentable;
b)
\hat{T}^{\star}
is a finitary Cartesian monad (and hence

\mathfrak{U}^{\star}{O}
is monadic)
c)
\mathfrak{U}^{\star}{O}
is finitary
d)
\mathfrak{U}^{\star}_{\kappa}
is monadic and finitary.

\lrcorner

Our main definition in the
T^{\star}
case, is in perfect analogy with Leinsteir’s definition
2.29
:

Definition 3.18
.

A
weak involutive globular
\omega
-category
is an algebra for an initial object
L^{\star}
in
\scr{O}^{T^{\star},\kappa}_{\bullet}
.

Remark 3.19
.

It is perfectly possible to utilize the category
\scr{O}\scr{K}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}
introducted in remark
3.10
instead of
\scr{O}^{\hat{T}^{\star},\kappa}_{\bullet}
in order to define a slightly more restrictive notion of involutive weak globular
\omega
-category as an algebra for the initial object in
\scr{O}\scr{K}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}
.

The existence of such initial object can be obtained with techniques perfectly similar to those utilized in theorems
3.12
and in the proof of theorem
3.16
, just adding to the family
\mathcal{X}
in equation (
3.47
) all the pairs of terms of
\mathfrak{M}(Q)
required for the validity of the additional axioms imposed by the commuting diagrams (
3.25
) and quotienting for the smallest congruence of
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operadic contraction containing
\mathcal{X}
(such minimal congruence always exists because
\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
is a terminal object in
\scr{O}\scr{K}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}_{\bullet}
).

\lrcorner

3.3
Examples

The strategy used to provide examples of weak involutive globular
\omega
-categories is perfectly parallel to the one described in section
2.3.4
and consists in producing:
a contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operad
P^{\star}
and an algebra
X^{\star}
over it.

We just mention here some immediate available examples of involutive weak globular
\omega
-categories.

\blacktriangleright

strict involutive globular
\omega
-categories
:
again, these coincide with algebras for the monad given by the terminal contracted-
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operad
\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)\in\scr{O}^{\hat{T}^{\star},\kappa}_{\bullet}
described in remark
3.11
.

\blacktriangleright

globular
\omega
-spans
: following the notation introduced in
[
Bertozzini Conti Lewkeeratiyutkul Suthichitranont 2020
, example 4.7]
we define an
\omega
-span as a sequence
(A^{n}\xleftarrow{t^{n}}Q^{n+1}\xrightarrow{s^{n}}B^{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
of 1-spans, where
Q^{n}:=A^{n}\cup B^{n}
; a globular
\omega
-span is an
\omega
-span
Q:=(Q^{n+1}\rightrightarrows Q^{n})
that is a globular
\omega
-set (in this way any
x\in Q^{n}
determines, with all its sources/targets a unique globular
n
-cell).
Introducing level-by-level the equivalence relation that identifies
(n+1)
-cells having the same source/target sets, we obtain a quotient
\omega
-globular set
X
. A family of globular
\omega
-spans is hence determined by the quotient morphism
Q\xrightarrow{\chi}X
.
Considering such family
Q\xrightarrow{\chi}X\xrightarrow{!}\bullet
as “generators”, we apply the free involutive magma functor (as constructed in point a. of the proof of proposition
3.2
) to get
\mathfrak{M}(Q)\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{M}(\chi)}\mathfrak{M}(X)\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{M}(!)}\mathfrak{M}(\bullet)
, applying the forgetful functor to the category of globular
\omega
-sets (that we omit to indicate) and using the quotient projection (constructed in point b. of proposition
3.2
) onto
\hat{T}^{\star}(Q)\xrightarrow{\hat{T}^{\star}(\chi)}\hat{T}^{\star}(X)\xrightarrow{\hat{T}^{\star}(!)}\hat{T}^{\star}(\bullet)
, we obtain a morphism of
\hat{T}^{\star}
-collections as in the first diagram below:

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern The
involutive weak globular
\omega
-category of globular
\omega
-spans generated by
Q\xrightarrow{\chi}X
is given by the morphism of free globular contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operads to the right of the diagram above (considered as algebras over themselves). Keeping track of the projection onto
X
and
\mathfrak{P}(X)
is necessary to “coarse grain” recovering the original spans and operations between them.

\blacktriangleright

homotopy
\omega
-groupoids

\Pi_{\omega}(X)
of a topological space
X
: here, since the involutions coincide with the weak inverse homotopies, one just show that the contracted
\hat{T}
-operad utilized in
[
Leinster 2004
, example 9.2.7]
is actually a contracted
\hat{T}^{\star}
-operad.

4
Outlook

The construction of algebraic involutive versions of weak (globular)
\omega
-categories (either in Penon’s or in Leinster’s approaches), as done in our previous work
[
Bejrakarbum Bertozzini 2017
]
and in the present paper, is only the very first step in the direction of a full operator algebraic categorical environment suitable for the needs of
categorical non-commutative geometry

[
Bertozzini Conti Lewkeeratiyutkul 2008
]
,
[
Bertozzini Conti Lewkeeratiyutkul 2012
]
.

Involutions, in the case of (weak) cubical
\omega
-categories, are currently under investigation,
27
27

27

Bejrakarbum P, Bertozzini P, Theesoongnern S,
Involutive Weak Cubical/Globular
\omega
-categories
(work in progress).

following recent work by
[
Kachour 2022
]
,
including the study of conditions (see
[
Al-Agl Brown, Steiner 2002
]
) assuring the equivalence between cubical and globular
involutive
settings, extending previous still unpublished results already achieved in the case of involutive 2-categories / double categories
[
Bertozzini Conti Dawe Martins 2014
]
.

Immediate further developments of the present work will concentrate on possible algebraic definitions and examples of involutive weak
\omega
-algebroids; subsequently the treatment of uniform structures related to completeness and norms necessary for the formulation of weak
\omega
-C*-algebroids will have to be addressed, generalizing (and possibly modifying) the strict
n
-C*-categorical notions tentatively put forward in
[
Bertozzini Conti Lewkeeratiyutkul Suthichitranont 2020
, section 5]
.

In the present paper, for simplicity, we have only considered monads and operads that do not possess involutive symmetries, but it is already evident that certain “covariant” involutions could have been introduced at the level of the operads in
\scr{O}^{\hat{T}^{\star}}
. Certain involutive monads and operads (see
[
Yau 2020
, chapter 4]
and references therein) can be used for this purpose. A full treatment of
involutive bicategories
and the discussion of
covariant vs contravariant involutive monads and operads in a bicategory
will be separately addressed in a companion paper
28
28

28

Bejrakarboom P, Bertozzini P, Puttirungroj C,
Involutive Monads and Hybrid Categories
(work in progress).

making direct use of hybrid-categories as put forward in
[
Bertozzini Puttirungroj 2014
]
.

In this work we have not considered any relaxing of the usual axioms for globular higher categories, in particular we did not formulate a definition of weak involutive globular
\omega
-categories with
non-commutative exchange property
, as already proposed in
[
Bertozzini Conti Lewkeeratiyutkul Suthichitranont 2020
, section 3.3]
for strict globular
n
-categories. We suspect that treatments of versions of
non-commutative derived geometries
(homotopies, cobordisms, holonomies) will require some axiomatic modification in such direction.
